#### BC-1.4.035: Keyring-first OAuth token storage with DPAPI-encrypted-file fallback on `keyring::Error::TooLong` (Windows only); backend-selection-level atomicity + rollback

**Confidence**: HIGH
**Source**: ADR-0021 §1/§2; `.factory/research/win-oauth-keychain-blob-limit-2026-09-03.md`; `src/api/auth.rs::store_oauth_tokens` (F4 target)
**Subject**: Auth & Identity
**Origin**: NEW (cycle-004 `windows-correctness`, DEC-334, ADR-0021 §1/§2)

**Description**: `store_oauth_tokens(profile, access, refresh)` continues to attempt `keyring::Entry::set_password` first for both the access and refresh token, unchanged on macOS/Linux and for any Windows secret that fits Credential Manager's 2560-byte blob ceiling. On Windows, when a `set_password` call returns `keyring::Error::TooLong(_, _)`, the WHOLE access+refresh pair is routed to a new DPAPI-encrypted-file store as a single atomic unit — never split across two backends. This closes #759 (OAuth login deterministically fails on Windows for any token exceeding ~1280 UTF-16 code units) at its root.

**Preconditions**:
1. `store_oauth_tokens` is called with a profile and a non-empty access+refresh token pair (post successful Atlassian token exchange or refresh-rotation).
2. Running on any platform — this BC's routing predicate is compiled into every platform; only the Windows DPAPI code path actually engages (Invariant 3).

**Postconditions**:
1. Both `set_password` calls succeed (small-secret case, all platforms) → pair stored in keyring exactly as before this cycle; any stale DPAPI file for this profile from a prior oversized-token generation is best-effort removed (never fails the call if removal fails).
2. Access `set_password` succeeds, refresh `set_password` returns `TooLong` → **before** calling `auth_windows_store::store_pair`, the profile's ENTIRE existing keyring pair — BOTH `<profile>:oauth-access-token` (including the just-written new value) AND `<profile>:oauth-refresh-token` (whatever value, if any, was already stored there) — is deleted via `delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry`, tolerating `NoEntry` on either key; only THEN is the whole pair written via `auth_windows_store::store_pair`. This supersedes a narrower "roll back just the just-written access key" framing (Pass-3 adversarial review Finding #1): deleting only the access key while leaving a pre-existing, untouched refresh key in place would strand a stale keyring remnant rather than fully clearing the backend. Never left with access-in-keyring/refresh-nowhere, and never left with a stale single remnant key in keyring after the fresh pair lands in the DPAPI file.
3. Access `set_password` itself returns `TooLong` → refresh is never attempted against keyring at all; **before** calling `auth_windows_store::store_pair`, the profile's ENTIRE existing keyring pair (both `<profile>:oauth-access-token` and `<profile>:oauth-refresh-token`, whichever are present) is deleted via `delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry`, tolerating `NoEntry` on either key; only THEN does the whole pair route directly to `auth_windows_store::store_pair`. A pre-existing, FITTING pair from a prior, smaller-token generation is exactly the vulnerable case this delete closes (Pass-3 adversarial review Finding #1): without it, this arm never touches keyring at all, leaving a stale-but-complete pair sitting there indefinitely.
4. Any other `Err` (genuine lock/permission/backend error, not `TooLong`) → propagates unchanged; no DPAPI engagement.
5. `auth_windows_store::store_pair` failing (disk full, DPAPI syscall failure, permission denied on the secrets directory) surfaces the `DpapiFallbackFailed` marker error (BC-1.4.039).
6. **Ordering, and why — delete-keyring-first, then DPAPI-store, never the reverse (Pass-3 adversarial review Finding #1).** Both `TooLong` arms above (Postconditions 2 and 3) perform the keyring-pair delete BEFORE calling `auth_windows_store::store_pair`, never after. This ordering is crash-safety-driven: if the process is killed in the window between the two steps, the delete has already completed and the DPAPI store has not yet run — the profile is left with NEITHER backend holding a pair, which `load_oauth_tokens` (BC-1.4.036) reports as a clean "No stored OAuth token" state, correctly prompting a fresh, unambiguous re-login. The reverse ordering (store-then-delete) would instead risk the opposite crash window — a crash after a successful DPAPI store but before the keyring delete — leaving BOTH backends holding a complete pair (the fresh DPAPI one and the stale keyring one) at the same time: exactly the shadowing defect this ordering exists to prevent, since `load_oauth_tokens`'s both-keys-present-in-keyring fast path (BC-1.4.036 Postcondition 1) never even attempts the DPAPI check once it finds both keyring keys present, and so would return the stale keyring pair forever.

**Invariants**:
1. The access/refresh pair is, at all times, either FULLY in the keyring or FULLY in one DPAPI file — never one secret in each backend. **Additionally, a profile's keyring pair and DPAPI-file pair are NEVER simultaneously both complete for the same profile (Pass-3 adversarial review Finding #1): after any successful `store_oauth_tokens` call, at most one of the two backends holds a usable (complete, two-key) pair for that profile.** This is a distinct guarantee from the first: the first prevents a pair being SPLIT across backends (one secret in each); this second half additionally prevents a STALE, complete pair surviving untouched in one backend while a FRESH, complete pair is written to the other. This is a postcondition of `store_oauth_tokens` specifically — enforced there, at write time, via Postcondition 6's delete-first ordering — not merely a general property asserted in isolation: `load_oauth_tokens`'s both-keys-present-in-keyring fast path (BC-1.4.036 Postcondition 1) is exactly the mechanism that turns a violation of this half into a real, user-visible defect — silent, permanent authentication against stale/rotated tokens, invisible to the user until the profile is manually cleared.
2. Routing decisions are made ONLY on the typed `keyring::Error::TooLong` variant returned by `keyring`'s own pre-flight validation — never a pre-flight length guess against a hardcoded byte budget (avoids duplicating/hardcoding `CRED_MAX_CREDENTIAL_BLOB_SIZE` outside `keyring`'s own check and drifting silently if `keyring` changes its ceiling or encoding).
3. **In RELEASE (production) builds, the DPAPI fallback is STRUCTURALLY unreachable on macOS and Linux by construction, not merely unlikely to engage (Pass-1 adversarial review Finding #5).** `store_oauth_tokens`'s `TooLong` match arms never call `should_fallback_to_dpapi` directly; they call a dedicated call-site wrapper, `engage_dpapi_fallback(err: &keyring::Error) -> bool` (`src/api/auth.rs`), which is itself `#[cfg(windows)]`-gated: on Windows it delegates to `should_fallback_to_dpapi`; on `#[cfg(not(windows))]`, in a RELEASE build, it is a hardcoded `always false`, unconditionally, regardless of the error variant. **Pass-6 adversarial review, Finding #1 (ADR-0021 §1): in a `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` build ONLY, the `#[cfg(not(windows))]` arm ADDITIONALLY honors the `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` debug-only test seam — when that env var is set to `1`, `engage_dpapi_fallback` returns `should_fallback_to_dpapi(err)` — i.e. `true` IFF `err` is `keyring::Error::TooLong`, and `false` for every other `keyring::Error` variant — purely for testability (it is what makes `store_oauth_tokens`'s DPAPI-routing dispatch reachable in default CI for VP-AUTHDX-011/012/022). Corrected by Pass-7 adversarial review, Finding #1 (ADR-0021 §1's authoritative code): the seam does NOT make `engage_dpapi_fallback` return `true` unconditionally/regardless of the error variant — it only lifts the `#[cfg(not(windows))]` hardcoded-`false` so `should_fallback_to_dpapi`'s `TooLong` predicate can be evaluated at all on non-Windows; a non-`TooLong` `Err` reaching this call site under the seam still returns `false` and propagates unchanged with NO DPAPI engagement (Postcondition 4), identically to the no-seam/release-build case. This seam is compiled out entirely in release builds (the `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` gate itself), so it has no bearing on production behavior; absent the seam — i.e. in every release build unconditionally, and in a debug build with the env var unset — `engage_dpapi_fallback` on `#[cfg(not(windows))]` remains hardcoded `false`.** Consequently, in a RELEASE build, a `keyring::Error::TooLong` arising on macOS/Linux — however unlikely a given backend is to produce one — is matched and propagated EXACTLY as it was before this cycle: `auth_windows_store::store_pair`/`load_pair` are NEVER reached, no DPAPI file-store function performs any file I/O, and `DpapiFallbackFailed` (BC-1.4.039) can NEVER be produced by `store_oauth_tokens` on a non-Windows release build. `should_fallback_to_dpapi` itself remains a pure, reachable-in-source, cross-platform-testable predicate — but it is `engage_dpapi_fallback`'s call-site gate, not `should_fallback_to_dpapi`'s own logic, that makes non-engagement on non-Windows a STRUCTURAL guarantee in release builds. Production behavior on macOS/Linux is byte-for-byte UNCHANGED by this cycle, as a consequence of this gating, not an incidental property to be verified only under normal (non-error) conditions; the debug-only seam carries no production-behavior implication of its own. **Scope clarification (Pass-5 adversarial review, Finding #2; ADR-0021 §9).** This invariant governs the DPAPI-fallback ENGAGEMENT decision only (`engage_dpapi_fallback`/`should_fallback_to_dpapi`'s `TooLong`-routing behavior) — it says nothing about, and does NOT extend to, and is NOT violated by, the profile-name path-traversal guard of BC-1.4.040 (`reject_unsafe_profile_component`), which is deliberately new, cross-platform hardening applying identically on every OS. A malformed or hostile profile name (a path separator, a drive-letter colon, a reserved device name) being rejected consistently on macOS/Linux exactly as it is on Windows is the INTENDED, in-scope outcome of BC-1.4.040 — not a regression this invariant forbids. A future reader (or adversarial pass) must not read "byte-for-byte unchanged" above as grounds to "fix away" or flag BC-1.4.040's non-Windows rejection.

**Edge Cases**:
- EC-1.4.035-1: Both access and refresh individually fit keyring but the SEQUENCE of two independent `set_password` calls is interrupted between them by a process kill (not a `TooLong` failure) — this is the PRE-EXISTING partial-write risk (unrelated to this BC's DPAPI routing); see BC-1.4.028 (partial-state handling, AMENDED this cycle) for the read-side recovery contract.
- EC-1.4.035-2: A previously-oversized refresh token shrinks after rotation (now fits keyring) on a profile whose pair currently lives in the DPAPI file — the NEXT successful keyring-first write per Postcondition 1 removes the now-stale DPAPI file as a best-effort side effect; a failure to remove it does not fail the overall store call (the DPAPI file simply becomes an orphaned, harmless artifact until the next successful cleanup attempt).
- EC-1.4.035-3: Running on Windows in a headless CI user context — `should_fallback_to_dpapi`'s predicate and the routing logic are exercised identically to an interactive session; only the real DPAPI syscall reachability in that context is unverified pending an F4 spike (architecture-delta §9 item 3).
- EC-1.4.035-4: A profile already has a COMPLETE, FITTING pair in the keyring from a prior, smaller-token generation, and a subsequent `store_oauth_tokens` call (e.g. a token refresh producing a larger token) hits `TooLong` on either the access or the refresh write (Pass-3 adversarial review Finding #1, STALE-KEYRING-SHADOWS-DPAPI) — without Postcondition 6's delete-first ordering, the pre-existing keyring pair would be left untouched (access-`TooLong` arm) or partially untouched (refresh-`TooLong`-after-access-succeeded arm) while the fresh pair lands in the DPAPI file, producing two simultaneously-complete/near-complete backends for one profile. Postconditions 2/3/6 close this: the existing keyring pair is deleted before the DPAPI store runs, so at most one backend ever holds a usable pair afterward.

**Verification Properties**:
- **VP-AUTHDX-011 — `TooLong`-triggered DPAPI fallback routing (SAFETY INVARIANT, cross-platform, NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 VP-delta pass; F1-provisional ID drafted here).** Property (two parts): (1) `should_fallback_to_dpapi(err)` returns `true` for EXACTLY the `keyring::Error::TooLong(_, _)` variant and `false` for EVERY other `keyring::Error` variant — a pure predicate, exhaustively checkable against the full `keyring::Error` variant set with no I/O; (2) `store_oauth_tokens`'s routing, driven by a `set_password` fault-injection seam that returns a chosen `keyring::Error`, selects the DPAPI `store_pair` path IFF a `TooLong` occurs — access-`TooLong` → whole pair to DPAPI with refresh NEVER attempted against keyring (Postcondition 3); access-OK + refresh-`TooLong` → access write rolled back then whole pair to DPAPI (Postcondition 2); any non-`TooLong` `Err` → propagates unchanged with NO DPAPI engagement (Postcondition 4); both-OK → keyring, no DPAPI (Postcondition 1). Routing is NEVER decided by a pre-flight length guess against a hardcoded byte budget (Invariant 2). **Seam dependency (CI-classification corrected Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #1; STATE-assertion core re-classified KEYRING-GATED Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #1):** sub-property (1) — the pure `should_fallback_to_dpapi` predicate — runs in default CI on ANY host with NO seam and NO keychain (it is a pure function over a constructed `keyring::Error`). Sub-property (2)'s routing DISPATCH — that `store_oauth_tokens` ACTUALLY calls `auth_windows_store::store_pair` on a `TooLong`, rolls back the access write, and never attempts the refresh write against keyring — has TWO gating dependencies, not one: **(i)** the `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`-gated `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` opt-in test seam must be set so `engage_dpapi_fallback` returns `true` on a non-Windows build (ADR-0021 §1; WITHOUT it the DPAPI-routing match arm is dead code on any non-Windows runner), AND **(ii)** a REAL OS keychain must be available, because the seam lifts ONLY the cfg-gate on `engage_dpapi_fallback` — it does NOT seam out `store_oauth_tokens`'s real keychain touchpoints: `store_oauth_tokens` drives `entry(&key)?.set_password(...)` and, on the delete-first ordering (BC-1.4.035 Postconditions 2/3/6), `delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry` against the OS keychain, and observing the rollback/no-split outcome means PRE-SEEDING and then RE-READING keyring state. Exactly as cycle-003's VP-AUTHDX-005/006/007 document, the keyring MOCK cannot persist state across `Entry::new()` and default Linux CI has no Secret Service backend, so sub-property (2)'s state/ordering assertions cannot run in default `cargo test` — they are **KEYRING-GATED** (`#[ignore]` + `JR_RUN_KEYRING_TESTS=1`, ADDITIONALLY + `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` on non-Windows; on Windows the real keychain + real DPAPI make the seam unnecessary), mirroring the VP-AUTHDX-005/006/007 boundary. The prior "sub-property (2)'s routing dispatch runs in default CI with only `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1`" framing OVERSTATED default-CI coverage: the seam alone is necessary but NOT sufficient — a real keychain is the second, independent requirement. Production/release behavior is unchanged either way (still hardcoded `false` absent the seam). **Verification method**: sub-property (1) — `proptest` over synthesized `keyring::Error` values, fully cross-platform, default CI, no seam, no keychain, no DPAPI syscall; sub-property (2)'s routing/rollback/no-split state assertions — unit/integration test with a `set_password` fault-injection seam producing `TooLong` against a pre-seeded pair on a REAL keychain, **KEYRING-GATED** (`#[ignore]` + `JR_RUN_KEYRING_TESTS=1`, + `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` on non-Windows; Windows via real keychain), NOT default CI. **Test-serialization requirement (Pass-6 adversarial review Finding #4).** Because `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` is a process-global environment variable, and this VP's seam-engaged (`=1`) sub-property (2) asserts an outcome OPPOSITE to the env-UNSET legacy "Unlock your keychain" test (VP-AUTHDX-017 / BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3's own test guidance) from the SAME `engage_dpapi_fallback` call site, every test that sets, reads, or unsets `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` MUST be serialized via an `env_lock`-style `std::sync::Mutex` held for the duration of the env-var-dependent section — exactly the `JR_SERVICE_NAME` pattern in `tests/oauth_refresh_integration.rs`, NOT a test-binary-wide `--test-threads=1` (which would over-serialize unrelated tests). The SAME mutex governs the env-UNSET legacy-message counterpart, so the two opposing-outcome classes can never interleave under `cargo test`'s default parallelism. **F6 target**: `src/api/auth_windows_store.rs::should_fallback_to_dpapi`; `src/api/auth.rs::store_oauth_tokens`; `src/api/auth.rs::engage_dpapi_fallback` (the `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` seam gating sub-property (2)'s default-CI reachability).
- **VP-AUTHDX-013 — Cross-platform non-engagement of `#[cfg(windows)] mod dpapi` (COMPILE-TIME / cfg-ABSENCE PROOF, NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 VP-delta pass; F1-provisional ID drafted here).** Property: on `#[cfg(not(windows))]` compilation units, (a) the `dpapi::{protect, unprotect}` FFI functions do not exist in the compiled artifact at all (they live inside a `#[cfg(windows)] mod dpapi`), and (b) the non-Windows arms of `store_pair`/`load_pair`/`remove_if_present` perform NO file I/O — **for any profile name that PASSES `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (BC-1.4.040 Postcondition 8)** — `load_pair` returns `Ok(None)`, `remove_if_present` returns `Ok(())`, and `store_pair` returns the `DpapiFallbackFailed` error, each immediately — so macOS/Linux behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged by this cycle for those (guard-passing) names (Invariant 3). **This no-op / `Ok(None)` / `Ok(())` claim is NOT blanket for every profile name (Pass-9 adversarial review, Finding #1 (VP portion), aligning this oracle with VP-AUTHDX-016(d) and BC-1.4.036 Postcondition 4 / EC-1.4.036-7 as qualified):** BC-1.4.040 Postcondition 8 makes `file_path(profile)? → reject_unsafe_profile_component` the mandatory FIRST statement of all three functions' `#[cfg(not(windows))]` arms too (Pass-4 Finding #2, ADR-0021 §3/§9), so a GUARD-REJECTING profile name (e.g. a Unix profile literally named `con`, or containing `:`/`..`/a path separator) returns `Err(ProfilePathEscape)` on macOS/Linux — NOT `Ok(None)`/`Ok(())`/`DpapiFallbackFailed` — before any no-op short-circuit is reached, exactly as VP-AUTHDX-016(d)'s guard-WIRING oracle asserts on the Linux CI runner and consistent with BC-1.4.036 EC-1.4.036-7. What THIS VP verifies is unchanged — the cfg-ABSENCE of `dpapi::*` on non-Windows (a) and the byte-for-byte-unchanged legacy no-op behavior for VALID (guard-passing) names (b); the carve-out only prevents (b) from contradicting the guard-rejection branch shared with VP-016(d) and the qualified BCs. **Verification method**: `cfg`-gated absence proof — a `#[cfg(not(windows))]` test asserting the no-op return shapes of `load_pair`/`remove_if_present`/`store_pair` (each asserted with a guard-PASSING profile name; a guard-REJECTING name yields `Err(ProfilePathEscape)` on every OS instead — that branch is VP-AUTHDX-016(d)'s wiring oracle, not this VP), plus a `cfg`-audit that no `dpapi::` symbol is referenced outside a `#[cfg(windows)]` block. This is a compile-time / return-shape proof of ABSENCE, NOT a runtime behavioral test of the Windows path. **F6 target**: `src/api/auth_windows_store.rs` (`#[cfg(not(windows))]` arms of `store_pair`/`load_pair`/`remove_if_present`; the `#[cfg(windows)] mod dpapi` gate).
- **VP-AUTHDX-022 — Stale-keyring-shadow closure: a pre-existing COMPLETE keyring pair is deleted BEFORE the DPAPI fallback store, so a subsequent load never returns the stale pair (SAFETY INVARIANT, HIGH PRIORITY, keyring-gated routing/delete-ordering core + Windows-only real-DPAPI tail, NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 Pass-3 adversarial-review follow-on — Finding #1, STALE-KEYRING-SHADOWS-DPAPI; CI-classification corrected from "cross-platform default-CI core" to KEYRING-GATED Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #1).** Unit under test: `store_oauth_tokens`'s delete-keyring-first-then-DPAPI-store routing (BC-1.4.035 Postconditions 2/3/6, Invariant 1's second clause, EC-1.4.035-4) on a profile that ALREADY holds a COMPLETE, FITTING keyring pair from a prior, smaller-token generation — the precise case every OTHER VP in this delta misses (they exercise either a fresh profile with no pre-existing pair to shadow, or the non-`TooLong` happy path). Property — given a pre-seeded complete keyring pair `(old_access, old_refresh)` and a fresh `store_oauth_tokens(profile, new_access, new_refresh)` whose keyring `set_password` returns `keyring::Error::TooLong`, tested as TWO distinct cases (both identified as vulnerable by Finding #1): (i) the ACCESS write overflows — access-`TooLong` arm, Postcondition 3; and (ii) the access write succeeds but the REFRESH write overflows — refresh-`TooLong`-after-access-succeeded arm, Postcondition 2 — assert in BOTH cases that after `store_oauth_tokens` returns `Ok`: (a) BOTH namespaced keyring keys (`<profile>:oauth-access-token` AND `<profile>:oauth-refresh-token`) are ABSENT — the ENTIRE pre-existing stale keyring pair is deleted (both keys, tolerating `NoEntry`), NOT merely the just-written key, superseding the narrower "roll back just the access key" framing that would strand a stale remnant (Postcondition 2/3); (b) the DPAPI file holds the FRESH pair `(new_access, new_refresh)`; and (c) a subsequent `load_oauth_tokens(profile)` returns the FRESH DPAPI pair, NEVER the stale `(old_access, old_refresh)` keyring values — i.e. NO shadowing through `load_oauth_tokens`'s both-keys-present-in-keyring fast path (BC-1.4.036 Postcondition 1). **Delete-FIRST ordering, and its crash-safety corollary (Postcondition 6):** additionally assert the keyring pair is deleted BEFORE `auth_windows_store::store_pair` is invoked, never after — a fault injected in the window between the keyring delete and the DPAPI store (a `store_pair` that returns an error / the `JR_S303_PERSIST_FAIL`-style seam) leaves NEITHER backend holding a usable pair (both keyring keys absent, no DPAPI file present), which `load_oauth_tokens` reports as a clean "No stored OAuth token" state — never the stale keyring pair, and never the reverse (store-then-delete) window where both backends momentarily hold a complete pair. **Coverage boundary (KEYRING-GATED routing/delete-ordering core; Windows-only real-DPAPI round-trip tail — seam dependency for the core added Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #1, STATE-persistence dependency corrected from "default CI" to KEYRING-GATED Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #1):** the delete-keyring-first ordering, the both-keys-absent assertion (a), and the "neither backend populated on a mid-window fault" crash-safety property are all unit/integration-testable via a `set_password` fault-injection seam producing `TooLong` on a PRE-SEEDED complete keyring pair — but this core has TWO gating dependencies, not one, and is NOT default-CI-reachable off-Windows: **(i)** the `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`-gated `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` test seam (ADR-0021 §1) must be engaged, WITHOUT which `engage_dpapi_fallback` returns `false` on a non-Windows build and `store_oauth_tokens` never enters the delete-then-DPAPI-store arm at all (the whole match arm is dead code off-Windows); AND **(ii)** a REAL OS keychain is required, because the seam lifts ONLY the `engage_dpapi_fallback` cfg-gate — it does NOT seam out `store_oauth_tokens`'s real `set_password`/`delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry` keychain calls, and the property's whole substance is PRE-SEEDING a complete keyring pair and then RE-READING keyring state to prove both keys became ABSENT. Exactly as cycle-003's VP-AUTHDX-005/006/007 document, the keyring MOCK cannot persist state across `Entry::new()` and default Linux CI has no Secret Service backend, so this state/ordering core CANNOT run in default `cargo test`. It is **KEYRING-GATED** (`#[ignore]` + `JR_RUN_KEYRING_TESTS=1`, ADDITIONALLY + `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` on non-Windows; on Windows the real keychain + real DPAPI make the seam unnecessary), mirroring the VP-AUTHDX-005/006/007 boundary. WITH both dependencies satisfied on a non-Windows keyring-gated run, because `auth_windows_store::store_pair` returns `DpapiFallbackFailed` immediately, the run ITSELF exercises the crash-window shape (keyring deleted, DPAPI store fails → neither backend populated), and `store_oauth_tokens` returns `Err` — so the keyring-gated core is precisely the delete-first + neither-backend-on-fail "delete-then-fail" shape; the property statement's full "(a)+(b)+(c) after `store_oauth_tokens` returns `Ok`" SUCCESS oracle additionally requires a real, successful `store_pair` and is therefore the Windows-only real-DPAPI tail. This VP has NO pure-default-CI portion of its own: its entire property is state-persistence-dependent, so the earlier "default-CI-reachable core" framing OVERSTATED coverage — the seam made the arm REACHABLE on non-Windows, but reaching it still needs a real keychain to observe the state it asserts. Sub-assertions (b) "DPAPI file holds the fresh pair" and (c) "subsequent load returns the fresh DPAPI pair, not the stale keyring pair" require the real DPAPI `store_pair`/`load_pair` round-trip and so run on a Windows CI runner — OR cross-platform via a `load_pair` injection seam manufacturing the `Ok(Some(fresh_pair))` outcome (mirroring VP-AUTHDX-015's seam-based load-branch coverage) — the same Windows-only-tail-on-a-cross-platform-core split as VP-AUTHDX-012/018. **Verification method**: unit/integration test with a `set_password` fault-injection seam returning `keyring::Error::TooLong` against a pre-seeded complete keyring pair — asserting both-keys-deleted + delete-before-store ordering + neither-backend-on-mid-window-fault (**KEYRING-GATED**: `#[ignore]` + `JR_RUN_KEYRING_TESTS=1`, + `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` on non-Windows, NOT default CI, because both a real keychain — to pre-seed and re-read the pair — and the seam are required; without the seam the whole arm is unreachable off-Windows, and without a real keychain the pre-seed/re-read state is unobservable per the VP-AUTHDX-005/006/007 keyring-mock boundary), and the fresh-DPAPI-pair-loads-not-stale round-trip (Windows CI, or cross-platform via a `load_pair` seam, still keyring-gated for the stale-pair pre-seed). **Test-serialization requirement (Pass-6 adversarial review Finding #4).** Because `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` is a process-global environment variable, and this VP's seam-engaged (`=1`) delete-then-fail test asserts an outcome OPPOSITE to the env-UNSET legacy "Unlock your keychain" test (VP-AUTHDX-017 / BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3's own test guidance) from the SAME `engage_dpapi_fallback` call site, every test that sets, reads, or unsets `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` MUST be serialized via an `env_lock`-style `std::sync::Mutex` held for the duration of the env-var-dependent section — exactly the `JR_SERVICE_NAME` pattern in `tests/oauth_refresh_integration.rs`, NOT a test-binary-wide `--test-threads=1` (which would over-serialize unrelated tests). The SAME mutex governs the env-UNSET legacy-message counterpart, so the two opposing-outcome classes can never interleave under `cargo test`'s default parallelism. **F6 target**: `src/api/auth.rs::store_oauth_tokens` (delete-keyring-first routing on BOTH `TooLong` arms); `src/api/auth.rs::load_oauth_tokens` (fresh-DPAPI-pair-not-stale-keyring on a subsequent load); `src/api/auth.rs::engage_dpapi_fallback` (the `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` seam gating the default-CI core's reachability).
- **VP-AUTHDX-023 — `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` debug-seam release-gate: the seam's env-var read is compiled OUT of release builds (RELEASE-GATE / cfg-ABSENCE PIN, cross-platform / Windows-COMPILED-buildable, default CI, spike-independent — same class as VP-AUTHDX-010(a), NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 Pass-6 adversarial-review follow-on — Finding #3; ADR-0021 §1's expanded doc-fallout note).** This is a build/test-scaffolding property of the seam introduced by Pass-5 (BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3 / ADR-0021 §1), NOT a behavioral property of `store_oauth_tokens` — it does not overlap any other VP's oracle. `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` gates a security-critical credential-storage routing decision (the DPAPI-fallback dispatch), so the established `JR_*` debug-only-seam convention (CLAUDE.md "AI Agent Notes") requires the same release-gate proof every sibling seam ships. Property (two parts): **(a)** a dedicated `tests/jr_force_dpapi_fallback_release_gate.rs` source-text-scan pin asserts `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` appears within 5 source lines of the `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` env-var read in `src/api/auth.rs`'s `#[cfg(not(windows))] fn engage_dpapi_fallback` — modeled byte-for-byte on the sibling-seam convention (`tests/jr_test_block_until_sigint_release_gate.rs` / `tests/config_dir_release_gate.rs`), a NEW file per the one-file-per-seam rule; **(b)** the seam is compiled out of release builds entirely — in a release build (and in a debug build with the env var unset) `engage_dpapi_fallback` on `#[cfg(not(windows))]` stays hardcoded `false`, so production non-Windows routing is byte-for-byte unchanged (BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3). Sub-property (a) is what turns (b) from an assertion resting on the seam's current source shape into a proven, regression-caught property. **Verification method**: a source-text-scan test — no runtime behavior exercised, no keychain, no DPAPI syscall — asserting the `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`-proximity property; cross-platform, default CI, spike-independent (requires only that `src/api/auth.rs` exists, same host-independence as every sibling `*_release_gate.rs`), and it must SHIP IN THE SAME COMMIT that introduces the seam (ADR-0021 §1's three-part doc-fallout checklist: this test + the CLAUDE.md `JR_*` seam-table entry + the explicit "compiled out of release" statement in both places). **F6 target**: `tests/jr_force_dpapi_fallback_release_gate.rs` (NEW); `src/api/auth.rs::engage_dpapi_fallback` (the `#[cfg(not(windows))]` arm's `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`-gated `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` env read).

**Related BCs**: BC-1.4.036 (read-path DPAPI-file fallback, the sibling of this BC's write path), BC-1.4.037 (the atomic file-write mechanism `store_pair` delegates to), BC-1.4.038 (delete-both-backends), BC-1.4.039 (honest-fail message when `store_pair` itself fails), BC-1.4.028 (pre-existing partial-write recovery, AMENDED this cycle), BC-1.4.025 (OAuth legacy-flat-key migration — confirmed UNCHANGED regression baseline, not touched by this BC), BC-6.1.004 (`validate_profile_name` charset/length/reserved-name rejection — the PRIMARY, live gate BC-1.4.040's defense-in-depth guard sits behind; Pass-20 gate-audit correction), BC-6.1.005 (validation runs at config-load, both strict and lenient loaders, and at the CLI-flag/resolved-active-profile-name boundary; Pass-20 gate-audit correction).

**Architecture Anchors**: ADR-0021 §1/§2; architecture-delta.md §1.2/§2.1/§3 (`store_oauth_tokens` row).

**Story Anchor**: TBD (F3)

**VP Anchors**: VP-AUTHDX-011, VP-AUTHDX-013, VP-AUTHDX-022 (stale-keyring-shadow closure — Postconditions 2/3/6, Invariant 1's second clause, EC-1.4.035-4; NEW cycle-004 F2 Pass-3 follow-on), VP-AUTHDX-023 (`JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` debug-seam release-gate — Invariant 3's `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` seam compiled out of release; NEW cycle-004 F2 Pass-6 follow-on, Finding #3), VP-AUTHDX-012 (declared at BC-1.4.037; co-covers this BC's no-split-across-backends guarantee — Invariant 1 / Postcondition 2 — per the vp-delta coverage map)

**Trace**: F2 spec evolution, cycle-004 `windows-correctness` (2026-09-03), issue #759, DEC-334; ADR-0021 §1/§2; architecture-delta.md §1.2/§2.1/§3/§6; cross-reference BC-1.4.028 (AMENDED — the pre-existing partial-write scope), BC-1.4.025 (confirmed-unchanged regression baseline). Pass-5 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-03): Finding #2 (MED) — Invariant 3 gains a scope-clarification sentence: the invariant governs DPAPI-fallback ENGAGEMENT only and does not forbid, and is not violated by, BC-1.4.040's intentional, new, cross-platform profile-name-rejection guard (ADR-0021 §9). Pass-6 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-03): Finding #1 (HIGH) — Invariant 3 reworded to scope the "macOS/Linux hardcoded false, unconditionally" claim to RELEASE builds specifically, and to state the `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`-gated `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` test seam (ADR-0021 §1) that VP-AUTHDX-011/012/022 already depend on for default-CI reachability — closing a contradiction where this Invariant's prose still described the pre-seam function, making those VPs' seam-based coverage claim un-runnable-per-spec. Pass-7 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-03): Finding #1 (MED) — Invariant 3's seam description corrected to match ADR-0021 §1's authoritative code exactly: under the seam, `engage_dpapi_fallback` returns `should_fallback_to_dpapi(err)` (true IFF `TooLong`), NOT `true` unconditionally/regardless of the error variant — the prior wording, read literally, would have routed a non-`TooLong` error to DPAPI in a debug+seam build, contradicting VP-AUTHDX-011 Postcondition 4's "any non-`TooLong` `Err` propagates unchanged, no DPAPI engagement" assertion. Pass-8 adversarial review VP follow-on (formal-verifier, 2026-09-03): Finding #1 (HIGH, CI-classification honesty) — VP-AUTHDX-011's sub-property (2) routing/rollback STATE core and VP-AUTHDX-022's ENTIRE routing/delete-ordering core RE-CLASSIFIED from "cross-platform default CI (with `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1`)" to **KEYRING-GATED** (`#[ignore]` + `JR_RUN_KEYRING_TESTS=1`, + `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` on non-Windows): the seam lifts only `engage_dpapi_fallback`'s cfg-gate and does NOT seam out `store_oauth_tokens`'s real `set_password`/`delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry` keychain calls, and the keyring MOCK cannot persist the pre-seed→re-read state these oracles assert (the VP-AUTHDX-005/006/007 boundary). Only VP-AUTHDX-011's pure `should_fallback_to_dpapi` predicate (sub-property (1)) remains default-CI, no seam, no keychain. No VP added/removed (cumulative VP count stays 55); oracle CI-classification only. #### BC-1.4.036: OAuth token load path checks the DPAPI-encrypted file when both namespaced keyring keys are absent; a corrupt file yields a distinct force-re-login error

**Confidence**: HIGH
**Source**: ADR-0021 §4; `src/api/auth.rs::load_oauth_tokens` (F4 target)
**Subject**: Auth & Identity
**Origin**: NEW (cycle-004 `windows-correctness`, DEC-334, ADR-0021 §4)

**Description**: `load_oauth_tokens(profile)` gains one new branch, inserted where today's code reaches the "both namespaced keys absent" case, before falling through to the existing `"default"`-only legacy-flat-key recovery (BC-1.4.025, unchanged). A token stored via EITHER backend (keyring or the DPAPI file) loads successfully and identically from the caller's perspective.

**Preconditions**:
1. `load_oauth_tokens(profile)` is called (directly, or transitively via `refresh_oauth_token_with_url`/`JiraClient::from_config`/`JiraClient::send`'s 401-reconcile path).

**Postconditions**:
1. Both namespaced keyring keys present → return them (fast path, unchanged, all platforms).
2. Both namespaced keyring keys absent → attempt `auth_windows_store::load_pair(profile)`. On any `Err(e)`, discrimination is now FOUR-WAY, checked in this order (Pass-5 adversarial review, Finding #2; ADR-0021 §4 as amended): **`ProfilePathEscape` FIRST**, then the pre-existing three-way split (b)/(c)/(d) below:
   - `e.downcast_ref::<ProfilePathEscape>()` is `Some` (the profile name itself fails `reject_unsafe_profile_component`, BC-1.4.040) → propagate a distinct exit-64 `JrError::UserError` naming the invalid profile — verbatim: `"Profile name {profile:?} is not valid for jr's Windows encrypted-file OAuth-token fallback storage ({reason}) — this restriction applies only to that fallback (used when an OAuth token is too large for Windows Credential Manager), not to keyring-based credential storage in general. If your OAuth token is, or was, too large for the keyring, re-authenticate under a different profile name: \"jr auth login --oauth --profile <new-name>\"."`, where `{reason}` is a short, variant-specific phrase derived from the `ProfilePathEscape` variant (Pass-11 adversarial review Finding #2, ADR-0021 §4 as amended — this message deliberately does NOT assert the credentials are currently in the keyring; at this read-path call site both namespaced keyring keys are already known absent, so it states only the general fallback-scope mechanism fact plus a conditional remediation) — NEVER (b)'s corruption/force-re-login wording below and NEVER (c)'s generic backend/IO wording below. This is required by BC-1.4.040 Postcondition 6 and was previously unreachable: without this check FIRST, a `ProfilePathEscape` — indistinguishable BY TYPE from any other `load_pair` failure unless explicitly downcast-checked — would fall through to (c)'s generic backend/IO branch and render a misleading "check file permissions" message that never names the actual problem (an invalid profile name).
   - (else — i.e. only once `ProfilePathEscape` has been ruled out) discrimination proceeds to (a)-(d):
   a. `Ok(Some((access, refresh)))` → return them; caller cannot distinguish this from a keyring-backed load.
   b. `Err(e)` where `e` is a GENUINE CORRUPT-ENVELOPE condition (bad envelope magic/version, malformed JSON, DPAPI decrypt failure, or a file created by a different Windows user account — i.e. the file was successfully OPENED/READ but its CONTENT is unusable) → propagate a DISTINCT, actionable FORCE-RE-LOGIN error: `"OAuth credentials for profile {profile:?} could not be decrypted (the file may be corrupted, or was created by a different Windows user account). Run \"jr auth login --oauth --profile {profile}\" to re-authenticate."` — this is NEVER coerced into "no stored OAuth token," which would misleadingly suggest the user never logged in.
   c. `Err(e)` where `e` is a GENUINE BACKEND/IO condition on an EXISTING file — permission-denied opening/reading the file, or a transient I/O error (disk error, file locked by another process) distinct from the file simply not existing (`Ok(None)` is the not-exists case, Postcondition 2d) — MUST NOT be reported via Postcondition 2b's corruption/force-re-login message, and MUST NOT capture a `ProfilePathEscape` case either (that is routed to the dedicated invalid-profile-name branch above, checked before this branch is ever reached — Pass-5 adversarial review, Finding #2): the file's CONTENT was never examined, so "corrupted" would misdiagnose a permission/IO problem as a credential problem and send the user toward an unnecessary re-login when the actual fix is a filesystem permission/availability issue. This mirrors the write path's typed distinction (BC-1.4.039's `DpapiFallbackFailed` vs. genuine keyring lock/permission errors): `load_pair`'s `Err` variant is TYPED so `load_oauth_tokens` can branch on "the profile name itself is invalid" vs. "content is corrupt" vs. "backend/IO failure reading an otherwise-present file" rather than collapsing every `Err` into one message. The backend/IO case propagates as a distinct, non-corruption error naming the underlying I/O failure (not the "may be corrupted" wording and never the invalid-profile-name wording), so the user is not misdirected toward re-authenticating, or toward choosing a different profile name, for a problem neither action can fix.
   d. `Ok(None)` (no file present — genuine absence, not an error) → fall through to the existing `"default"`-only legacy-flat-key check (BC-1.4.025, unchanged), then the existing "No stored OAuth token" error (unchanged).
3. Exactly one namespaced keyring key present (partial write) → existing logic is EXTENDED, not replaced (see BC-1.4.028, AMENDED): first the existing `"default"`-only legacy-pair recovery runs (unchanged); if that doesn't resolve it, additionally check `auth_windows_store::load_pair(profile)`, branching on the SAME typed `Err` distinction Postcondition 2 makes rather than collapsing every non-`Ok(Some)` outcome into the partial-keychain error (Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #1 — closing an asymmetry where Postcondition 2 drew this distinction but Postcondition 3 previously did not; see Invariant 1). As of Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #2, this includes checking `ProfilePathEscape` FIRST here too, for the identical reason Postcondition 2 does: `e.downcast_ref::<ProfilePathEscape>()` is `Some` → propagate the SAME distinct exit-64 invalid-profile-name error as Postcondition 2's dedicated branch — NEVER (b)'s corruption message below and NEVER today's "OAuth keychain entries … are partial" error. Only once `ProfilePathEscape` has been ruled out does discrimination proceed to (a)-(d):
   a. `Ok(Some((access, refresh)))` → PREFER it over raising the partial-state error (emitting a stderr warning that a partial keyring remnant was ignored) — unchanged from the prior behavior.
   b. `Err(e)` where `e` is a GENUINE CORRUPT-ENVELOPE condition (identical criteria to Postcondition 2b) → propagate the SAME DISTINCT force-re-login error as Postcondition 2b (naming the profile, "could not be decrypted … Run \"jr auth login --oauth --profile {profile}\"") — NEVER coerced into today's "OAuth keychain entries … are partial" error, which would misdiagnose an unusable corrupt DPAPI file as a merely-partial keyring state the legacy recovery could have resolved.
   c. `Err(e)` where `e` is a GENUINE BACKEND/IO condition on an EXISTING file (identical criteria to Postcondition 2c) → propagate the SAME DISTINCT non-corruption backend/IO error as Postcondition 2c, naming the underlying I/O failure — NEVER coerced into Postcondition 2b's corruption message, NEVER today's "OAuth keychain entries … are partial" error, and NEVER the invalid-profile-name branch above (that is checked first and, if it matched, this branch is never reached — Pass-5 adversarial review, Finding #2), since none of those is the correct diagnosis for a permission/availability failure.
   d. `Ok(None)` (genuine absence — no DPAPI file exists) → this is the ONLY `load_pair` outcome that falls through to today's "OAuth keychain entries … are partial" error, unchanged.
4. On macOS/Linux, `auth_windows_store::load_pair` always returns `Ok(None)` immediately — no file I/O attempted; Postcondition 2's branch is a permanent no-op there — **for any profile name that PASSES `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (BC-1.4.040 Postcondition 8).** This is NOT a blanket claim for every profile name on every OS (Pass-9 adversarial review, Finding #1, correcting an overstatement that contradicted this BC's own Postcondition 2/EC-1.4.036-7): BC-1.4.040 Postcondition 8 makes `file_path(profile)? → reject_unsafe_profile_component` the mandatory FIRST statement of `load_pair`'s `#[cfg(not(windows))]` arm too, so a GUARD-REJECTING profile name (e.g. a Unix profile literally named `con`, or containing `:`/`..`/a path separator) returns `Err(ProfilePathEscape)` on macOS/Linux — NOT `Ok(None)` — before any no-op short-circuit is reached. This is exactly what makes EC-1.4.036-7 and VP-AUTHDX-016(d)'s guard-wiring oracle (which calls `load_pair` on the Linux CI runner and asserts `Err(ProfilePathEscape)`) reachable on a non-Windows CI runner. The no-op is therefore: "no file I/O is ever attempted on macOS/Linux for a guard-passing profile name" — not "load_pair always returns Ok(None) on macOS/Linux."
5. **Precedence-ordering rule (both-absent vs. partial cases deliberately differ — do not "fix" this into uniformity):** Postcondition 2 (BOTH namespaced keys absent) checks the DPAPI file BEFORE the legacy `"default"`-only flat-key fallback, because in the both-absent case there is no ambiguity to resolve — the DPAPI file is the more likely and more specific source of an intact credential for a Windows profile that used the new fallback, and checking it first avoids a spurious "No stored OAuth token" detour through legacy-key logic that cannot possibly apply (the namespaced keys, not the legacy flat keys, are what BC-1.4.025's migration path targets). Postcondition 3 (EXACTLY ONE namespaced key present — a partial-write state) checks the EXISTING legacy `"default"`-only recovery FIRST, before DPAPI, because that recovery path is PRE-EXISTING, UNCHANGED (BC-1.4.028) behavior this cycle must not reorder or risk regressing — DPAPI is consulted only as a NEW, ADDITIONAL fallback after the established recovery has already had its unmodified chance to resolve the partial state. The rule, stated once: **new-path-first when there is no pre-existing recovery to preserve (Postcondition 2); pre-existing-recovery-first, new-path-as-additional-fallback when there is one (Postcondition 3).** This asymmetry is intentional and must not be collapsed into a single fixed check-order without re-deriving both rationales.

**Invariants**:
1. A corrupt/undecryptable DPAPI file is NEVER silently treated as "no token," and an invalid profile name is NEVER silently treated as a corrupt file, a backend/IO error, or "no token" — the distinctions between "never logged in," "logged in, but the stored credential is now unusable," "a backend/IO failure occurred reading an otherwise-present file," and "the profile name itself is invalid for credential storage" are preserved across both storage backends, AND across BOTH branches that consult `load_pair` — the both-keys-absent branch (Postcondition 2) and the exactly-one-key-present partial-write branch (Postcondition 3) — applying the identical, now FOUR-WAY, typed distinction with `ProfilePathEscape` checked FIRST, then corrupt-vs-backend/IO-vs-genuine-absence, regardless of which keyring state triggered the check (Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #1, closing an asymmetry where Postcondition 3 previously collapsed all three non-`Ok(Some)` outcomes into the single "partial keychain" error; Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #2, adding `ProfilePathEscape` as the FIRST-checked member of the distinction so it is never captured by the generic backend/IO branch).
2. `auth status`'s presence check (`load_oauth_tokens(&profile).is_ok()`) reports "authenticated" identically whether the token lives in keyring or the DPAPI file.
3. A genuine backend/IO error reading an EXISTING DPAPI file (permission-denied, transient disk/I/O failure) is NEVER misreported as corruption requiring re-login, in EITHER the both-absent branch (Postcondition 2c) or the partial-write branch (Postcondition 3c) — `load_pair`'s `Err` is typed so `load_oauth_tokens` can distinguish "content is corrupt" from "the backend/IO layer failed to read an otherwise-present file," mirroring the write path's typed `DpapiFallbackFailed`-vs-ordinary-error distinction (BC-1.4.039 Invariant 2). **Symmetrically (Pass-5 adversarial review, Finding #2): a genuine backend/IO error is also NEVER misreported as an invalid profile name, and a `ProfilePathEscape` is NEVER misreported as a backend/IO error or as corruption** — `load_pair`'s `Err` is typed so `load_oauth_tokens` distinguishes "the profile name itself is invalid" from "content is corrupt" from "the backend/IO layer failed to read an otherwise-present file" as three genuinely distinct diagnoses, with `ProfilePathEscape` checked FIRST at every call site so it can never fall through to either of the other two.

**Edge Cases**:
- EC-1.4.036-1: A profile's DPAPI file exists but was created under a DIFFERENT Windows user account (e.g. a config directory copied between machines/users) — `CryptUnprotectData` fails because DPAPI keys are user-scoped; this is indistinguishable, from `jr`'s perspective, from ordinary corruption, and surfaces the identical Postcondition 2b message (both are "force re-login," not a more specific "wrong user" diagnosis — over-specifying the cause risks a misleading claim `jr` cannot actually verify).
- EC-1.4.036-2: Both a namespaced-partial keyring state AND a complete, valid DPAPI file exist simultaneously (an unexpected dual-state that Postcondition 1 of BC-1.4.035 should prevent in normal operation, but which can arise from manual keychain edits or an interrupted rollback) — Postcondition 3a above prefers the DPAPI file and warns, rather than erroring outright.
- EC-1.4.036-3: The three read-call sites in `src/api/client.rs` (`from_config`, and the two `send()` 401-reconcile call sites) all observe identical behavior via the shared `load_oauth_tokens` function — no call-site-specific change is needed or permitted.
- EC-1.4.036-4: The DPAPI file exists and is well-formed, but reading it fails due to a permission-denied condition on the `secrets/<profile>/` directory or file, or a transient disk/I/O error — the file's CONTENT is never examined in this case → Postcondition 2c applies: this is reported as a distinct backend/IO error, NEVER coerced into Postcondition 2b's "could not be decrypted … re-authenticate" corruption message, since re-authenticating would not fix a filesystem permission or availability problem.
- EC-1.4.036-5 (Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #1): A namespaced-partial keyring state (exactly one key present) coexists with a CORRUPT/undecryptable DPAPI file — the unchanged legacy `"default"`-only recovery runs first; if it does not resolve the partial state, the corrupt DPAPI file is detected and Postcondition 3b's force-re-login error fires, NOT today's "OAuth keychain entries … are partial" error — a corrupt DPAPI file is a more specific, more actionable diagnosis than "partial keychain," so it takes precedence once `load_pair` is reached.
- EC-1.4.036-6 (Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #1): A namespaced-partial keyring state coexists with a DPAPI file that fails to read for backend/IO reasons (permission-denied, transient disk error) — same precedence order as EC-1.4.036-5, but the resulting error is Postcondition 3c's distinct non-corruption backend/IO error, never the corruption message and never the generic partial-keychain error.
- EC-1.4.036-7 (Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #2): `load_pair` returns `Err` where the profile name itself fails `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (BC-1.4.040) — `ProfilePathEscape` is checked FIRST, before the corrupt-envelope and backend/IO discrimination, in BOTH the both-absent branch (Postcondition 2) and the partial-write branch (Postcondition 3) → the distinct exit-64 invalid-profile-name error fires, NEVER Postcondition 2b/3b's corruption message and NEVER Postcondition 2c/3c's generic "check file permissions" backend/IO message.

**Verification Properties**:
- **VP-AUTHDX-015 — Read-path: a present-but-undecryptable DPAPI file is a distinct force-re-login error, never coerced into "no token"; a backend/IO error on an existing file is NEVER misreported as corruption (SAFETY INVARIANT, NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 VP-delta pass; typed backend/IO-vs-corruption distinction added Pass-1 adversarial review Finding #4).** Property: for `load_oauth_tokens(profile)` with both namespaced keyring keys absent — (a) a `load_pair` returning `Ok(Some((a, r)))` yields a result the caller CANNOT distinguish from a keyring-backed load (Postcondition 2a); (b) a `load_pair` returning `Err` where the error is a GENUINE CORRUPT-ENVELOPE condition (bad magic/version, malformed JSON, DPAPI decrypt failure, or a file created by a different Windows user — EC-1.4.036-1) propagates the DISTINCT force-re-login error naming "could not be decrypted … Run \"jr auth login --oauth --profile {profile}\"" and is NEVER coerced into the generic "No stored OAuth token" error (Postcondition 2b, Invariant 1); (c) a `load_pair` returning `Err` where the error is a GENUINE BACKEND/IO condition on an EXISTING file (permission-denied, transient I/O/disk error — content never examined) is NEVER routed through (b)'s corruption/force-re-login message — it propagates as a distinct, non-corruption error naming the underlying I/O failure, mirroring the write path's `DpapiFallbackFailed`-vs-ordinary-error typed distinction (BC-1.4.039) so a permission/availability problem is never misdiagnosed as "your credential is corrupt, re-authenticate" (Postcondition 2c); (d) a `load_pair` returning `Ok(None)` falls through to the existing `"default"`-only legacy recovery, then the unchanged "No stored OAuth token" error (Postcondition 2d). Additionally the AMENDED partial-state branch (exactly one namespaced key present, BC-1.4.028) applies the SAME typed `load_pair` distinction as the both-absent branch — NOT only the `Ok(Some)`-preferred case (Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #1, closing the asymmetry where Postcondition 3 previously collapsed every non-`Ok(Some)` outcome into the single "partial keychain" error): after the unchanged legacy-pair recovery runs first, (3a) `Ok(Some(pair))` is PREFERRED over raising the partial-state error (with a stderr warning, EC-1.4.036-2); (3b) an `Err` that is a GENUINE CORRUPT-ENVELOPE condition propagates the SAME distinct force-re-login "could not be decrypted … Run \"jr auth login --oauth --profile {profile}\"" error as Postcondition 2b — NEVER coerced into the "OAuth keychain entries … are partial" error (EC-1.4.036-5); (3c) an `Err` that is a GENUINE BACKEND/IO condition on an existing file propagates the SAME distinct non-corruption backend/IO error as Postcondition 2c, naming the underlying I/O failure — NEVER the corruption message and NEVER the partial-keychain error (EC-1.4.036-6, Invariant 3); and (3d) ONLY `Ok(None)` (genuine absence) falls through to the unchanged "OAuth keychain entries … are partial" error (Postcondition 3, Postcondition 3d). **Additionally — `ProfilePathEscape` RENDERING, checked FIRST (Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #2, BC-1.4.036 Postconditions 2/3, EC-1.4.036-7):** a `load_pair` `Err` whose chain contains a `ProfilePathEscape` marker (the profile name itself failed `reject_unsafe_profile_component`, BC-1.4.040) is discriminated BEFORE the corrupt-envelope (2b/3b) and backend/IO (2c/3c) checks, in BOTH keyring pre-states, and RENDERS as the distinct exit-64 invalid-profile-name `JrError::UserError` naming the profile — NEVER the "could not be decrypted"/force-re-login corruption message (2b/3b) and NEVER the generic "check file permissions"/"Unlock your keychain" backend/IO message (2c/3c). The oracle injects the `ProfilePathEscape`-carrying `load_pair` `Err` — the FIRST-checked member of BC-1.4.036's FOUR-WAY discrimination, not a separate "fifth" outcome — and asserts the invalid-profile-name RENDERING specifically, under both pre-states; this is a DISTINCT property from VP-AUTHDX-016 — 016 proves the guard EMITS `ProfilePathEscape` at `file_path`, whereas THIS asserts `load_oauth_tokens` RENDERS that marker correctly and first, so the two fail independently if either regresses. The oracle therefore injects all five `load_pair` results — the `Ok(Some)` success/fast path (2a/3a) plus BC-1.4.036's FOUR-WAY discrimination (`ProfilePathEscape` FIRST, then corrupt-envelope, then backend/IO, then genuine-absence `Ok(None)`) — under BOTH keyring pre-states — both-keys-absent (2a-2d) AND exactly-one-key-present (3a-3d) — and asserts the resulting error variant/text is IDENTICAL for the corrupt (2b≡3b) and backend/IO (2c≡3c) cases across the two branches, differing only in the `Ok(None)` fall-through target (2d's "No stored OAuth token" vs. 3d's "partial") — Invariant 1's cross-branch distinction-preservation. **Coverage boundary (split by keyring pre-state — both-absent branch DEFAULT CI, exactly-one-present partial-state branch KEYRING-GATED; Pass-12 adversarial review Finding #1, correcting an earlier "runs in default CI" claim that covered only the both-absent pre-state):** the error-mapping/branch-selection logic is unit-testable by injecting the four non-`ProfilePathEscape` `load_pair` outcomes (`Ok(Some)`/`Err`-corrupt/`Err`-backend-IO/`Ok(None)`) at the seam — but ONLY under the BOTH-ABSENT keyring pre-state (Postconditions 2a-2d) is this genuinely DEFAULT CI: the keyring mock supplies both-namespaced-keys-absent naturally, so injecting each `load_pair` outcome against that pre-state needs no keyring state persistence and runs in ordinary `cargo test` (constructed error values, no Windows/DPAPI/keychain dependency). Reaching the EXACTLY-ONE-KEY-PRESENT partial-state branch (Postconditions 3a-3d, the AMENDED BC-1.4.028 path) requires the keyring to PERSIST exactly one namespaced key across `Entry::new()` — the SAME VP-AUTHDX-005/006/007 state-persistence boundary — which the keyring mock cannot do and default Linux CI has no Secret Service backend for; that partial-state branch (including its `ProfilePathEscape`-first rendering under the one-key-present pre-state) is therefore **KEYRING-GATED** (`#[ignore]` + `JR_RUN_KEYRING_TESTS=1`; NO `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` seam is involved — this is the READ path, not the write/routing path), NOT default CI — Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #1 reclassified VP-AUTHDX-011/012's pre-seed→re-read STATE cores the same way, for the same reason. On macOS/Linux the real `load_pair` always returns `Ok(None)` for a profile name that PASSES `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (a guard-REJECTING name returns `Err(ProfilePathEscape)` on every OS instead — cross-ref VP-AUTHDX-016(d) / BC-1.4.036 EC-1.4.036-7), so the `Ok(Some)`/`Err` branches are proven via the seam rather than a real DPAPI file, and the corrupt-file `Err` value itself is manufacturable cross-platform via VP-AUTHDX-014's malformed-envelope path, while the backend/IO `Err` is manufacturable via a fault-injection seam on the file-open/read step distinct from envelope decoding. **Verification method**: the BOTH-ABSENT-branch half — a unit test injecting each `load_pair` outcome (including the `ProfilePathEscape`-carrying `Err` asserted to RENDER as the `ProfilePathEscape`-first invalid-profile-name message (Finding #2), never the corruption or backend/IO text) and asserting the resulting error variant/text (or success) — cross-platform, runs in DEFAULT CI (constructed error values, no Windows/DPAPI/keychain dependency); the EXACTLY-ONE-KEY-PRESENT partial-state-branch half — the same `load_pair`-outcome injection against a keyring pre-seeded with exactly one namespaced key — is **KEYRING-GATED** (`#[ignore]` + `JR_RUN_KEYRING_TESTS=1`), NOT default CI, because the keyring mock cannot persist the single-key pre-state (the VP-AUTHDX-005/006/007 boundary; Pass-12 Finding #1). **F6 target**: `src/api/auth.rs::load_oauth_tokens`.

**Related BCs**: BC-1.4.035 (the write-side counterpart), BC-1.4.037 (envelope encode/decode this BC's `load_pair` call depends on — VP-AUTHDX-014), BC-1.4.028 (AMENDED — partial-state handling this BC extends, co-covered by VP-AUTHDX-015), BC-1.4.025 (unchanged legacy-flat-key recovery this BC falls through to), BC-1.4.040 (source of the `ProfilePathEscape` marker this BC's read path now discriminates FIRST, Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #2), BC-6.1.004 (`validate_profile_name` — the PRIMARY, live gate that makes BC-1.4.040's `ProfilePathEscape` branch a defense-in-depth-only path; Pass-20 gate-audit correction), BC-6.1.005 (validation runs at config-load and the CLI-flag/resolved-active-profile-name boundary; Pass-20 gate-audit correction).

**Architecture Anchors**: ADR-0021 §4; architecture-delta.md §2.1/§3 (`load_oauth_tokens` row).
