#### BC-1.4.037: DPAPI-encrypted-file store: versioned envelope + atomic temp-write-fsync-and-rename; sole `unsafe` FFI surface in the module tree

**Confidence**: HIGH
**Source**: ADR-0021 §3; src/api/auth_windows_store.rs (NEW FILE — does not exist yet, F4 target, not a live citation)
**Subject**: Auth & Identity
**Origin**: NEW (cycle-004 `windows-correctness`, DEC-334, ADR-0021 §3)

**Description**: New sibling module `src/api/auth_windows_store.rs` (subsystem SS-03) implements the DPAPI-encrypted-file backend with a deliberate pure/impure seam: `envelope::{encode, decode, wrap, unwrap}` and `should_fallback_to_dpapi` (BC-1.4.035) are pure, cross-platform-testable functions; only the two-function `dpapi::{protect, unprotect}` FFI wrapper and the file write itself are Windows-only/impure. The on-disk file at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\jr\secrets\<profile>\oauth-tokens.dat` is written atomically: encode → DPAPI-protect → wrap → temp-write (same directory) → `rename` over the final path.

**Preconditions**:
1. `auth_windows_store::store_pair(profile, access, refresh)` is called (only ever from `store_oauth_tokens`'s routing logic, BC-1.4.035).
2. Running on `#[cfg(windows)]` for the real path; `#[cfg(not(windows))]` returns the `DpapiFallbackFailed` error immediately with no file I/O attempted (this arm exists only so the cross-platform call site compiles uniformly — the size threshold that triggers it is realistically never hit by macOS Keychain/Linux Secret Service) — **for any profile name that PASSES `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (BC-1.4.040 Postcondition 8).** This is NOT a blanket claim for every profile name (Pass-10 adversarial review, Finding #1, the same class of correction Pass-9 made to BC-1.4.036 Postcondition 4/BC-1.4.038 Invariant 2, propagated here): BC-1.4.040 Postcondition 8 makes `file_path(profile)? → reject_unsafe_profile_component` the mandatory FIRST statement of `store_pair`'s `#[cfg(not(windows))]` arm too, so a GUARD-REJECTING profile name returns `Err(ProfilePathEscape)` on macOS/Linux — NOT `DpapiFallbackFailed` — before this arm's own early return is reached.

**Postconditions**:
1. `envelope::encode(access, refresh)` produces a plaintext byte sequence (JSON: `{version, access, refresh}`) that `envelope::decode` parses back to the identical `(access, refresh)` pair, byte-for-byte, for any UTF-8 string pair including values well above the 2560-byte Credential Manager ceiling.
2. `envelope::wrap(protected_bytes)` prepends a 4-byte magic (`b"JROD"`) + 1-byte version to the DPAPI-protected ciphertext; `envelope::unwrap(file_bytes)` validates this 5-byte header and returns the remaining protected bytes, OR a distinct "unrecognized envelope" error for an unrecognized magic/version — never silently coerced into "no token."
3. **Atomicity claim, scoped precisely by failure class (Pass-1 adversarial review Finding #17):** the atomic file write builds the full file bytes in memory, writes to `<profile-dir>/oauth-tokens.dat.tmp-<random-suffix>` in the SAME directory as the final path (creating the directory via `create_dir_all` first if absent), `fsync`s the temp file's contents to disk, THEN `std::fs::rename`s over the final path. This distinguishes two failure classes: (a) **process-kill** (the `jr` process itself is terminated mid-write, OS and disk remain up) — covered unconditionally by the temp-write-and-rename sequencing alone: the FINAL path is left as either the OLD complete file (rename never reached) or the NEW complete file (rename succeeded) — never a truncated or partially-written file at the final path, regardless of whether fsync ran; (b) **power-loss** (the machine itself loses power before the OS has flushed the temp file's writes to durable storage) — covered ONLY by the added fsync-before-rename step: without it, a rename immediately following a buffered (not yet durable) write could, on some filesystems, survive a power-loss as a renamed-but-not-actually-persisted-to-disk file, or the rename operation's own durability could race the temp file's data; the fsync closes this gap by ensuring the temp file's bytes are durable BEFORE the rename is issued. The BC's "never leaves a truncated/partial file at the final path" guarantee is therefore process-kill-covered by construction (sequencing alone) AND power-loss-covered by the fsync addition — these are two distinct guarantees, not one, and must not be conflated. **Best-effort, AGE-GATED temp-file cleanup (Pass-3 adversarial review Finding #3, correcting a Pass-2 propagation gap — Pass-2 Finding #6 amended ADR-0021 §3 to age-gated cleanup but this BC's Postcondition 3 was never updated to match):** before writing a NEW temp file, `store_pair` makes a best-effort pass over `*.tmp-*` siblings left behind by a prior interrupted write in the same directory, removing ONLY those whose file-modification age exceeds `STALE_TMP_THRESHOLD` (30 seconds) — this is NOT a blanket delete of every `*.tmp-*` sibling. A `*.tmp-*` sibling younger than the threshold is deliberately left untouched: it is assumed to be another, concurrently-running `jr` process's own in-flight temp write for the same profile (e.g. a login racing a refresh), and deleting it would break that OTHER process's own temp-write-fsync-and-rename sequence out from under it — `refresh_coordinator.rs`'s single-flight protection is scoped to ONE process only and provides no cross-process locking here, so a blanket delete is unsafe. Only a genuinely ABANDONED temp file — old enough that no reasonable in-flight write could still be using it — is removed. A failure to clean up a stale (age-qualifying) temp file does not fail the current write (an orphaned `.tmp-*` file is harmless — it is never the target of any read path). This residual cross-process concurrency boundary is accepted as-is, not solved by adding new locking, and is explicitly out of DEC-334's scope.
4. `dpapi::protect`/`dpapi::unprotect` (the sole `unsafe` code in this module tree) call `CryptProtectData`/`CryptUnprotectData` with USER scope only — `CRYPTPROTECT_LOCAL_MACHINE` is never passed (that flag would let ANY local user on the machine decrypt the blob, defeating the point of a per-user OAuth secret).

**Invariants**:
1. `envelope::{encode, decode, wrap, unwrap}` and `should_fallback_to_dpapi` are deterministic, side-effect-free functions with no I/O or syscalls — unit-testable on any OS/CI runner (Pure Core, architecture-delta.md §6).
2. `dpapi::{protect, unprotect}` are the ONLY `unsafe` code in this module tree — two thin wrapper functions (build a `DATA_BLOB` from a Rust slice, call the FFI function, copy the output `DATA_BLOB` into an owned `Vec<u8>`, free the output buffer via `LocalFree`); no other file-write, JSON, or routing logic in this module is `unsafe`.
3. `windows-sys` 0.60.2 (already present transitively via `keyring`'s `windows-native` feature) is promoted to a direct `[target.'cfg(windows)'.dependencies]` entry — this introduces ZERO new dependency-graph nodes; the existing `deny.toml` `[[bans.skip]]` entry for `windows-sys` version `"0.60"` gains an updated `reason` field naming this module's DPAPI usage alongside keyring's `windows-native` feature, in the same change.

**Edge Cases**:
- EC-1.4.037-1: The `envelope::decode`/`unwrap` functions receive a structurally malformed byte sequence (bad JSON, missing field, wrong magic/version) → a distinct "corrupt envelope"/"unrecognized envelope" error is returned, never a panic and never silently coerced into an empty/absent result (this is the mechanism BC-1.4.036 Postcondition 2b's force-re-login message depends on).
- EC-1.4.037-2: Disk full, or the `secrets/<profile>/` directory is not writable (permission denied) during the temp-write step → the write fails BEFORE the `rename`, so the existing final-path file (if any) is completely untouched; this failure surfaces as `DpapiFallbackFailed` (BC-1.4.039).
- EC-1.4.037-3: A `CryptProtectData`/`CryptUnprotectData` syscall failure (Windows-only; e.g., DPAPI subsystem unavailable) is a distinct impure-shell failure from the pure envelope-format errors above; it too surfaces as `DpapiFallbackFailed` on the write path (BC-1.4.039) and as the corrupt-file force-re-login error on the read path (BC-1.4.036).

**Verification Properties**:
- **VP-AUTHDX-010 — DPAPI USER-scope flag invariant (Windows-COMPILED, spike-independent) + real `protect`/`unprotect` round-trip (Windows-RUNTIME) (SECURITY-CRITICAL, WINDOWS-ONLY, NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 VP-delta pass; F1-provisional ID drafted here; split into a compile-reachable flag-constant sub-property + a syscall-runtime sub-property so the single most security-critical DPAPI invariant is NOT left dependent on the open headless-reachability spike — cycle-004 F2 Pass-1 adversarial review Finding #10).** This VP has TWO sub-properties with DELIBERATELY DIFFERENT testability boundaries:
  - **(a) USER-scope-only flag invariant (the highest-value security property — Windows-COMPILED, does NOT require headless `CryptProtectData` runtime reachability):** `dpapi::protect` passes a `dwFlags` argument to `CryptProtectData` whose `CRYPTPROTECT_LOCAL_MACHINE` bit (`0x4`) is NEVER set — i.e. USER scope (Postcondition 4); passing `CRYPTPROTECT_LOCAL_MACHINE` would let ANY local user on the machine decrypt the per-user OAuth secret, defeating the point of the store. The current concrete `dwFlags` value is `CRYPTPROTECT_UI_FORBIDDEN` (`0x1`, the standard non-interactive/headless flag — ADR-0021 §8, Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #3), noted here NON-normatively so a reader can see what is actually passed; the PINNED assertion is deliberately NOT an exact-equality on the whole flag word but ONLY the security-relevant bit-clear property (`dwFlags & CRYPTPROTECT_LOCAL_MACHINE == 0`), so a future, unrelated flag addition to `dwFlags` does not force this VP to be re-pinned. This sub-property is asserted by a Windows-COMPILED unit test that pins the `dwFlags` value actually passed at the call site (a named `const DPAPI_PROTECT_FLAGS: u32` used as the sole `dwFlags` argument, asserted `& CRYPTPROTECT_LOCAL_MACHINE == 0` — the `LOCAL_MACHINE` bit clear, NEVER an exact `== 0` on the full word) — its coverage requires ONLY that the code compiles for the `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` target (`cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, or the unit test running on a `windows-latest` runner), and is INDEPENDENT of whether the DPAPI subsystem can actually be exercised headlessly. It therefore provides automated coverage of the never-`LOCAL_MACHINE` invariant regardless of the open F4 spike outcome below. **`CRYPTPROTECT_UI_FORBIDDEN` is INTENTIONALLY UNPINNED at the spec/VP level (deliberate scope choice, not a gap — cycle-004 F2 Pass-15 non-blocking observation, closed as documents-as-accepted):** sub-property (a) pins ONLY the security-relevant `LOCAL_MACHINE`-bit-clear property, so that a future unrelated flag addition to `dwFlags` does not force this VP to be re-pinned; no VP asserts that `dwFlags` actually SETS `CRYPTPROTECT_UI_FORBIDDEN`, so a regression dropping `UI_FORBIDDEN` (reopening the documented headless-hang risk — EC-1.4.035-3 / ADR-0021 §8) would be caught by no test here. This is accepted at the spec level; F4/F6 MAY add a cheap Windows-COMPILED presence assertion for `CRYPTPROTECT_UI_FORBIDDEN` on both `protect()` and `unprotect()` (e.g. `dwFlags & CRYPTPROTECT_UI_FORBIDDEN != 0`) if headless-hang regression protection is wanted — that would be an ADDITIVE test-scaffolding choice, not a change to this VP's pinned invariant.
  - **(b) Real round-trip (syscall-RUNTIME, spike-dependent):** on Windows, for any plaintext byte sequence `p` (including a `p` larger than the 2560-byte Credential Manager ceiling), `dpapi::unprotect(dpapi::protect(p)) == p` byte-for-byte, and the intermediate ciphertext is NOT equal to `p` (confirming protection actually occurred). This sub-property exercises the real `CryptProtectData`/`CryptUnprotectData` syscalls and so depends on runtime DPAPI reachability.
  **Coverage boundary (WINDOWS-ONLY for BOTH, but split by DEPENDENCY):** `#[cfg(windows)] mod dpapi` does not exist as compiled code on macOS/Linux (VP-AUTHDX-013), so neither sub-property runs on a macOS/Linux runner. Within Windows, however, they differ: sub-property (a) is a COMPILE-REACHABLE constant assertion that passes on any Windows build/unit run and does NOT need the DPAPI syscall to execute — so the never-`LOCAL_MACHINE` invariant has automated Windows coverage even if the headless spike is inconclusive; sub-property (b) is the SINGLE property in the cluster requiring the real syscalls to run, and runs on a Windows CI runner or via manual validation (the same shape of coverage boundary cycle-003 documented for its keyring-gated scenario, VP-AUTHDX-007). F1 §10 / architecture-delta §9 item 3 name whether `windows-latest` GitHub Actions can exercise `CryptProtectData` headlessly as an open F4 spike — that spike gates (b) ONLY, not (a). **F7/gate note:** the F7 delta-convergence gate MUST treat sub-property (a) as the required automated coverage of the never-`LOCAL_MACHINE` invariant and MUST NOT record this VP as entirely uncovered on an inconclusive (b) spike; if the headless spike fails, (b) falls back to a manual real-Windows smoke test while (a) remains automated. **Verification method**: (a) Windows-COMPILED unit test pinning the `dwFlags` security invariant (`dwFlags & CRYPTPROTECT_LOCAL_MACHINE == 0` — the `LOCAL_MACHINE` bit clear; NOT an exact `== 0` on the full word, since the current concrete value is `CRYPTPROTECT_UI_FORBIDDEN` = `0x1`, ADR-0021 §8) — automated, spike-independent; (b) Windows-only integration test (round-trip assertion), gated so it is a clean no-op on non-Windows, with a manual real-Windows smoke test as the fallback if the CI spike is inconclusive. **F6 target**: `src/api/auth_windows_store.rs::dpapi::{protect, unprotect}` (and the `dwFlags` constant `protect` passes to `CryptProtectData`).
- **VP-AUTHDX-012 — Backend-selection atomicity: the pair is never split, and the file write never partially lands (SAFETY-CRITICAL PROPERTY, NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 VP-delta pass; F1-provisional ID drafted here; age-gated stale-temp-cleanup sub-property (3) added cycle-004 F2 Pass-3 adversarial-review follow-on — Finding #3).** Three properties: (1) **no-split invariant (cross-platform):** for every `store_oauth_tokens` outcome (both-keyring-OK; access-OK/refresh-`TooLong`; access-`TooLong`), the resulting state is EITHER both secrets in the keyring OR both secrets in one DPAPI file — never access-in-keyring/refresh-in-DPAPI or any one-in-each combination; the access-OK-then-refresh-`TooLong` path rolls back the keyring access write (`delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry`) BEFORE routing the whole pair to DPAPI (BC-1.4.035 Postcondition 2, Invariant 1). (2) **atomic file write (sequencing cross-platform; real rename/fsync Windows-only; process-kill vs. power-loss scoped per Finding #17):** `store_pair` builds the full file bytes in memory, writes to `oauth-tokens.dat.tmp-<random-suffix>` in the SAME directory, `fsync`s it, then `rename`s over the final path — a fault injected between temp-write and rename (mirroring the existing `JR_S303_PERSIST_FAIL` fault-seam pattern) leaves the final path either the OLD complete file or absent, NEVER a truncated/partial file at the final path (Postcondition 3). This no-truncation guarantee holds unconditionally against PROCESS-KILL (sequencing alone); the added fsync-before-rename step extends it to POWER-LOSS durability specifically — these are the two distinct failure classes Postcondition 3 now names, not one undifferentiated "atomicity" claim. (3) **age-gated stale-temp cleanup — POSITIVE verification (cross-platform via a directory-scan seam; BC-1.4.037 Postcondition 3, Pass-3 adversarial-review Finding #3):** before writing a NEW temp file, `store_pair`'s best-effort cleanup pass over `*.tmp-*` siblings in the same directory removes ONLY those whose file-modification age EXCEEDS `STALE_TMP_THRESHOLD` (30 seconds) and PRESERVES any younger sibling — a `*.tmp-*` file younger than the threshold is assumed to be another concurrently-running `jr` process's own in-flight write for the same profile (a login racing a refresh; `refresh_coordinator.rs`'s single-flight is per-process only and provides no cross-process locking), so deleting it would break that other process's own temp-write-fsync-and-rename sequence. The oracle POSITIVELY verifies BOTH halves of the age gate, NOT merely that cleanup happens: against a directory pre-seeded with BOTH a FRESH (<30 s mtime) `*.tmp-*` sibling AND an AGED (>30 s mtime) `*.tmp-*` sibling, a `store_pair`/cleanup pass REMOVES the aged sibling while the fresh sibling is PRESERVED intact — never a blanket delete of all `*.tmp-*` siblings (the pre-fix behavior this closes), and never a delete of the fresh (in-flight) one. A failure to remove an age-qualifying stale temp never fails the current write (an orphaned `.tmp-*` file is harmless — it is never the target of any read path). **Coverage boundary (seam dependency corrected Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #1; no-split/rollback STATE core re-classified KEYRING-GATED Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #1):** the no-split routing / rollback-ordering LOGIC (delete `access_key`, delete `refresh_key`, then call `store_pair`, in that order, on either overflow arm) is a STATE property of `store_oauth_tokens` — proving it means observing that the keyring access key is rolled back and the refresh key never written, i.e. RE-READING keyring state after the call. It has TWO gating dependencies: **(i)** the `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`-gated `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` test seam (ADR-0021 §1) engaged — WITHOUT it, `engage_dpapi_fallback` returns `false` on a non-Windows build, so `store_oauth_tokens` never routes into the `store_pair`/rollback arm at all — AND **(ii)** a REAL OS keychain, because the seam lifts ONLY the `engage_dpapi_fallback` cfg-gate and does NOT seam out `store_oauth_tokens`'s real `set_password`/`delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry` keychain calls; exactly as cycle-003's VP-AUTHDX-005/006/007 document, the keyring MOCK cannot persist state across `Entry::new()` and default Linux CI has no Secret Service backend. The no-split/rollback state core is therefore **KEYRING-GATED** (`#[ignore]` + `JR_RUN_KEYRING_TESTS=1`, ADDITIONALLY + `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` on non-Windows; Windows via real keychain), NOT default CI — the prior "cross-platform in default CI with `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1`" framing OVERSTATED it (the seam is necessary but not sufficient; a real keychain is the second, independent requirement). The temp-then-rename SEQUENCING and the real `std::fs::rename` NTFS-atomicity + `fsync` durability steps are exercised on a Windows CI runner — the seam has NO bearing on that portion (it lives entirely inside `store_pair`'s `#[cfg(windows)]` arm, unreachable from a non-Windows build regardless of the seam). The age-gate cleanup of sub-property (3) is PLAIN FILESYSTEM logic (mtime read + `std::fs::remove_file`), independent of the DPAPI syscall AND of the keychain, so its positive fresh-preserved/aged-removed verification IS cross-platform-unit-testable in DEFAULT CI against a real temp directory pre-seeded with the two siblings — no seam, no keychain — PROVIDED the cleanup step is reachable independently of the `#[cfg(windows)]` DPAPI-protect step (e.g. a factored directory-scan helper); if instead the cleanup is inlined only within `store_pair`'s Windows path, this sub-property joins the Windows-CI tail alongside the real rename/fsync step. **Verification method**: the no-split/rollback state half — unit/integration test with a `set_password` fault-injection seam producing `TooLong` against a pre-seeded pair on a REAL keychain, **KEYRING-GATED** (`#[ignore]` + `JR_RUN_KEYRING_TESTS=1`, + `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` on non-Windows; Windows via real keychain), NOT default CI; the real temp-write/fsync/rename step Windows CI; the directory-scan age-gate cleanup test pre-seeding a fresh (<30 s) and an aged (>30 s) `*.tmp-*` sibling and asserting aged-removed/fresh-preserved — cross-platform, DEFAULT CI via the directory-scan helper (no keychain, no seam), else Windows CI if inlined in the Windows path. **Test-serialization requirement (Pass-6 adversarial review Finding #4).** Because `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` is a process-global environment variable, and this VP's seam-engaged (`=1`) no-split/rollback and temp-rename-sequencing tests assert an outcome OPPOSITE to the env-UNSET legacy "Unlock your keychain" test (VP-AUTHDX-017 / BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3's own test guidance) from the SAME `engage_dpapi_fallback` call site, every test that sets, reads, or unsets `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` MUST be serialized via an `env_lock`-style `std::sync::Mutex` held for the duration of the env-var-dependent section — exactly the `JR_SERVICE_NAME` pattern in `tests/oauth_refresh_integration.rs`, NOT a test-binary-wide `--test-threads=1` (which would over-serialize unrelated tests). The SAME mutex governs the env-UNSET legacy-message counterpart, so the two opposing-outcome classes can never interleave under `cargo test`'s default parallelism. (The sub-property (3) age-gate cleanup test needs no such serialization — it is plain-filesystem logic that never reads the seam.) **F6 target**: `src/api/auth.rs::store_oauth_tokens` (rollback / no-split); `src/api/auth_windows_store.rs::store_pair` (temp-write-fsync-and-rename; age-gated `*.tmp-*` cleanup + `STALE_TMP_THRESHOLD`).
- **VP-AUTHDX-014 — Versioned-envelope round-trip and corrupt/unrecognized-envelope rejection (SAFETY-CRITICAL PROPERTY, cross-platform / PURE, NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 VP-delta pass).** Two properties over the pure `envelope` functions: (1) **round-trip:** for ANY UTF-8 `(access, refresh)` pair — bounded generators producing realistic OAuth-token-shaped values including values well above the 2560-byte Credential Manager ceiling — `envelope::decode(envelope::encode(access, refresh)) == (access, refresh)` byte-for-byte, and `envelope::unwrap(envelope::wrap(protected)) == protected` for any protected-byte slice, with the 5-byte header (`b"JROD"` magic + 1-byte version) round-tripping exactly (Postcondition 1/2). (2) **corrupt/unrecognized → distinct `Err`, never coercion:** `envelope::decode` on structurally-malformed plaintext (bad JSON, missing `access`/`refresh` field) and `envelope::unwrap` on a byte sequence with an unrecognized magic or version each return a DISTINCT "corrupt envelope"/"unrecognized envelope" `Err` — NEVER a panic, and NEVER silently coerced into an empty/absent/`Ok(None)`-shaped result (EC-1.4.037-1; this is the mechanism BC-1.4.036 Postcondition 2b's force-re-login message depends on, consumed by VP-AUTHDX-015). **Verification method**: property test (`proptest`, bounded valid-credential generators for the round-trip half + arbitrary/adversarial byte generators for the corrupt-input half) — fully cross-platform, runs in default CI (pure byte transforms, no I/O or syscalls; Pure Core per architecture-delta §6). **F6 target**: `src/api/auth_windows_store.rs::envelope::{encode, decode, wrap, unwrap}`.

**Related BCs**: BC-1.4.035 (the routing logic that calls `store_pair`), BC-1.4.036 (the read-side `load_pair` counterpart — VP-AUTHDX-015 consumes VP-AUTHDX-014's corrupt-envelope proof), BC-1.4.040 (the secret-file PATH itself — profile-name sanitization, VP-AUTHDX-016, a distinct concern from this BC's envelope/atomicity contract).

**Architecture Anchors**: ADR-0021 §3/§5; architecture-delta.md §2.1 (module interface table), §5 (dependency graph), §6 (purity boundary map).

**Story Anchor**: TBD (F3)

**VP Anchors**: VP-AUTHDX-010, VP-AUTHDX-012, VP-AUTHDX-014

**Trace**: F2 spec evolution, cycle-004 `windows-correctness` (2026-09-03), issue #759, DEC-334; ADR-0021 §3/§5; architecture-delta.md §2.1/§5/§6; F1 delta analysis §6 (dependency assessment), §10 (Windows-only testability risk). Pass-8 adversarial review VP follow-on (formal-verifier, 2026-09-03): Finding #1 (HIGH, CI-classification honesty) — VP-AUTHDX-012's sub-property (1) no-split/rollback ORDERING STATE core RE-CLASSIFIED from "cross-platform default CI (with `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1`)" to **KEYRING-GATED** (same reasoning as BC-1.4.035's Trace note: the seam does not seam out `store_oauth_tokens`'s real keychain calls, and the keyring mock cannot persist the pre-seed→re-read state the rollback oracle asserts — VP-AUTHDX-005/006/007 boundary). VP-AUTHDX-012's sub-property (3) age-gated `*.tmp-*` cleanup REMAINS default CI (plain filesystem logic, no keychain, no seam); sub-property (2)'s real rename/fsync stays Windows-only. No VP added/removed (cumulative VP count stays 55); oracle CI-classification only. Companion record: `.factory/cycles/cycle-004/phase-f2-spec-evolution/vp-delta.md` (Pass-8 testability-tally correction). Pass-10 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-04): Finding #1 (MED) — Precondition 2's blanket "`#[cfg(not(windows))]` always returns the `DpapiFallbackFailed` error immediately" claim was overstated and contradicted VP-AUTHDX-016(d) and architecture-delta.md §2.1's `store_pair` row ("guard, THEN always … `DpapiFallbackFailed`"); reworded to qualify the claim as holding only for a profile name that PASSES `reject_unsafe_profile_component` — a guard-rejecting name returns `Err(ProfilePathEscape)` on every OS, before this arm's own early return. Same class of correction as Pass-9's BC-1.4.036/038 fix, propagated here. No postcondition/invariant renumbering; no VP change.

#### BC-1.4.038: `clear_profile_oauth_pair`/`clear_profile_creds` delete BOTH the keyring pair AND the DPAPI file

**Confidence**: HIGH
**Source**: ADR-0021 §7; `src/api/auth.rs::clear_profile_oauth_pair`, `::clear_profile_creds` (F4 targets)
**Subject**: Auth & Identity
**Origin**: NEW (cycle-004 `windows-correctness`, DEC-334, ADR-0021 §7)

**Description**: `clear_profile_oauth_pair` (used by `auth logout`, BC-1.2.013) and `clear_profile_creds` (used by `auth remove`, BC-1.2.014) each gain one additional step — `auth_windows_store::remove_if_present(profile)` — called ALONGSIDE (not instead of) the existing two keyring deletes, using the same `NotFound`-is-success tolerance already established for `delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry`. Without this, `logout`/`remove`/a mechanism switch would leave an orphaned encrypted secret file on disk after every other credential trace is gone.

**Preconditions**:
1. `clear_profile_oauth_pair(profile)` or `clear_profile_creds(profile)` is called for a profile that may or may not have an OAuth pair stored in either backend.

**Postconditions**:
1. `clear_profile_oauth_pair` deletes `<profile>:oauth-access-token`, `<profile>:oauth-refresh-token` (unchanged), AND `auth_windows_store::remove_if_present(profile)` — all three tolerate a not-found/absent target as success, not an error.
2. `clear_profile_creds` performs the same three deletions as part of its existing four-step delete (OAuth pair, API-token pair, cache directory, config entry) — credential deletion, INCLUDING the DPAPI file, happens before config-entry removal (consistent with BC-1.2.014's existing step-ordering contract).
3. After either function returns `Ok(())`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\jr\secrets\<profile>\oauth-tokens.dat` does not exist for that profile, regardless of which backend (keyring or DPAPI file) the pair was actually stored in at the time of the call.
4. **Ordering/error-fan-out contract (both functions):** every deletion step is ATTEMPTED, unconditionally — a failure on one step NEVER short-circuits the remaining steps. `clear_profile_oauth_pair` attempts both keyring deletes AND `remove_if_present` even if one of them returns a genuine (non-`NotFound`) error; `clear_profile_creds` attempts its full deletion set (OAuth pair's two keyring keys, `remove_if_present`, the API-token pair's two keyring keys, the cache directory) before touching the config entry, REGARDLESS of whether an earlier step in that set failed. `NotFound`/absent-target outcomes are tolerated (Invariant 1) and do not count as a failure for this purpose. If one or more steps return a genuine error, the FIRST such genuine error encountered (in the function's fixed attempt order) is the one PROPAGATED to the caller AFTER all attempts complete — never before. This prevents the failure mode where an early short-circuit on one backend's error leaves a live, undeleted remnant in another backend (e.g. a keyring error aborting the call before `remove_if_present` is ever attempted, leaving the DPAPI file on disk with no error surfaced about it) — the caller's `Err` result reflects that at least one step failed, but every step was still attempted regardless. **`remove_if_present`'s outcome is further discriminated before it can count as a "genuine error" for this fan-out (Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #3, ADR-0021 §7 as amended):** both `clear_profile_oauth_pair` and `clear_profile_creds` route the DPAPI-removal step through a dedicated caller-side adapter, `clear_dpapi_file_tolerating_path_escape(profile)`, rather than calling `auth_windows_store::remove_if_present` directly. That adapter maps a `ProfilePathEscape` result to `Ok(())` — TOLERATED, identically to `NotFound` — on EVERY OS, never propagated as the function's error and never counted as "the first genuine error" for this fan-out. This is provably safe, not merely convenient: `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (BC-1.4.040) is ALSO the mandatory first statement of `store_pair`, so no profile name this guard rejects could ever have had a DPAPI file successfully written for it by any version of `jr` carrying this guard — "the guard refuses to compute the path" and "no file exists under this name" are the same fact for such a profile. Any OTHER error from `remove_if_present` (a genuine filesystem error — e.g. permission denied) is NOT tolerated by this adapter and remains the propagated genuine error exactly as EC-1.4.038-3 already specifies; `remove_if_present`'s own signature, cfg-arm behavior, and mandatory-guard-first contract (Pass-4 Finding #2, ADR-0021 §9) are completely UNCHANGED by this adapter — only the CALLERS' classification of a `ProfilePathEscape` result changes.

**Invariants**:
1. `remove_if_present` NEVER fails the overall clear operation on a missing file — `NotFound` is success, mirroring the existing `delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry` idiom exactly.
2. On macOS/Linux, `remove_if_present` returns `Ok(())` immediately with no file I/O attempted — a permanent no-op there, consistent with BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3 — **for any profile name that PASSES `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (BC-1.4.040 Postcondition 8).** This is NOT a blanket claim for every profile name on every OS (Pass-9 adversarial review, Finding #1, the same class of correction Pass-8 already made for VP-011/012/022, propagated here): BC-1.4.040 Postcondition 8 makes `file_path(profile)? → reject_unsafe_profile_component` the mandatory FIRST statement of `remove_if_present`'s `#[cfg(not(windows))]` arm as well, so a GUARD-REJECTING profile name returns `Err(ProfilePathEscape)` on macOS/Linux — NOT `Ok(())` — before the no-op is reached. This is precisely what Postcondition 4's `clear_dpapi_file_tolerating_path_escape` adapter exists to tolerate, and only makes sense because `remove_if_present` CAN surface `ProfilePathEscape` for a guard-rejecting name on every OS, including non-Windows (see also Invariant 3 and EC-1.4.038-5, which depend on this outcome being reachable rather than impossible). The no-op is therefore: "no file I/O is ever attempted on macOS/Linux for a guard-passing profile name" — not "remove_if_present always returns Ok(()) on macOS/Linux."
3. **Defense-in-depth guarantee (Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #3, ADR-0021 §7 as amended; reclassified Pass-20 gate-audit correction, 2026-09-04).** A profile name that fails `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (BC-1.4.040) — reachable only via a future relaxation of `validate_profile_name`'s (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005) charset/reserved-name rules, or a validation-call-site regression bypassing that gate — continues to have its credentials FULLY cleared by `auth logout`/`auth remove`, on every OS including Windows. **This is NOT a backward-compatibility fix for a pre-existing, working profile that "predates this guard's introduction" (Pass-8's original framing) — no config `jr` has ever loaded could contain such a name, since `validate_profile_name` already rejects a colon or a reserved device-name stem at config-load, before any `auth` subcommand ever dispatches (ADR-0021 §7, Pass-20 gate-audit correction).** The guarantee is retained as defense-in-depth, symmetric with BC-1.4.040's own reclassification: BC-1.4.040's guard, deliberately hardened and applied identically on every OS for NEW writes/reads, must never block the cleanup of credentials under either of those two future-hypothetical scenarios.

**Unlike the store/read paths, this path never renders a `ProfilePathEscape` as a user-visible error (Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #2, dependent on Finding #3).** Unlike BC-1.4.036's read-path handling and BC-1.4.039's Sites 1/3 (which check `ProfilePathEscape` FIRST and render it as a distinct, user-visible exit-64 "invalid profile name" error for a NEW write/read), the clear path NEVER renders a `ProfilePathEscape` from `remove_if_present` as a user-visible error at all. Per the amended Postcondition 4 above, it is tolerated identically to `NotFound`. `auth logout`/`auth remove` have only two outcomes for this step: tolerated (no-op; includes both `NotFound` and `ProfilePathEscape`) or genuine-error (propagated, per EC-1.4.038-3). Cross-reference BC-1.4.039's Postcondition 1, where the same marker type is discriminated FIRST and rendered as a distinct error — the two BCs' differing treatment of the same marker type is deliberate and should be read side-by-side, not as a contradiction.

**Edge Cases**:
- EC-1.4.038-1: A profile whose pair lives entirely in the DPAPI file (never touched keyring) — `clear_profile_oauth_pair`'s two keyring deletes both hit `NotFound`/tolerate-absent, and the DPAPI file removal is the ONLY deletion that actually removes anything; the overall call still succeeds.
- EC-1.4.038-2: A profile with BOTH a stale/orphaned DPAPI file (from EC-1.4.035-2's cleanup-failure scenario) AND a currently-active keyring pair — both are removed; there is no scenario where clearing a profile's credentials leaves either backend's data behind.
- EC-1.4.038-3: A genuine filesystem error removing the DPAPI file (e.g., permission denied on the `secrets/` directory, distinct from "file does not exist") propagates as a real error from the clear function, rather than being silently swallowed — consistent with BC-1.2.014's existing "genuine keychain errors surfaced, not swallowed" contract (I-4/SR-008), now extended to this third backend; per Postcondition 4, this does NOT abort the remaining deletion steps — they are still attempted, and this error is propagated only after all attempts complete (first-genuine-error-wins if multiple steps fail).
- EC-1.4.038-4: A genuine keyring error deleting `<profile>:oauth-access-token` occurs FIRST in the attempt order, and the DPAPI file removal (a LATER step) would have failed too had the keyring error not been recorded → per Postcondition 4, the keyring error does not short-circuit the call; `remove_if_present` is still attempted (and, in this scenario, also fails or leaves a remnant), but the FIRST genuine error (the keyring one) is the error ultimately propagated to the caller — never a silent partial failure where one backend's remnant survives unreported because an earlier step's error ended the attempt sequence early.
- EC-1.4.038-5 (Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #3; reclassified Pass-20 gate-audit correction, 2026-09-04): A profile name that fails `reject_unsafe_profile_component` — e.g. one containing `:`, or named a reserved Windows device-name stem such as `con` — is cleared successfully by `auth logout`/`auth remove`: the DPAPI-removal step is tolerated as a no-op (never a genuine error, per the amended Postcondition 4), and every other deletion step (the keyring pair(s), the API-token pair for `clear_profile_creds`, the cache directory, the config entry) proceeds and completes normally, and the call returns `Ok`. This holds identically on Windows, macOS, and Linux. **Per the Pass-20 gate-audit correction (ADR-0021 §7): such a name is NOT reachable via any config `jr` has ever loaded** — `validate_profile_name` (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005) already rejects a colon or a reserved device-name stem at config-load, before any `auth` subcommand dispatches, so this edge case is reachable only under a future relaxation of that validation's charset/reserved-name rules or a validation-call-site regression bypassing it; the guarantee is retained as defense-in-depth for those two future-hypothetical scenarios, not as a fix for a pre-existing, working profile that predates this guard.
- EC-1.4.038-6 (Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #3; accepted residual, documented not engineered away): A DPAPI file that somehow exists on disk under a name the CURRENT guard would reject — reachable only via an out-of-band mechanism, a future guard-tightening applied after such a file already existed, or a defect in an earlier `jr` version that lacked this guard — is NOT found or removed by `remove_if_present`, since `file_path` fails the guard before any existence check runs. This is a narrow completeness gap only — it never affects the credential's CONFIDENTIALITY, since DPAPI still gates decryption regardless of whether the file is ever deleted — and mirrors the ADR's already-accepted stale-`*.tmp-*`-file residual (ADR-0021 §3). No test is required to exercise this residual; it is recorded as a documented, out-of-scope limitation, not asserted as a passing property.

**Verification Properties**:
- **VP-AUTHDX-018 — Delete-both-backends completeness AND attempt-all/first-error-propagated fan-out (SAFETY INVARIANT, NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 VP-delta pass; PROMOTED to a dedicated property at F2 despite being implementable as an F4 test extension, because credential-remnant survival after a `logout`/`remove` is a security-relevant completeness guarantee, not merely an example-based assertion; ordering/fan-out clause added Pass-1 adversarial review Finding #3; clear-path `ProfilePathEscape`-tolerance oracle assertions added Pass-8 adversarial review Findings #3/#2).** Property: after `clear_profile_oauth_pair(profile)` OR `clear_profile_creds(profile)` returns `Ok(())`, NEITHER backend retains the profile's OAuth pair — both namespaced keyring keys (`<profile>:oauth-access-token`/`-refresh-token`) are absent AND `auth_windows_store::remove_if_present(profile)` has been invoked such that `%LOCALAPPDATA%\jr\secrets\<profile>\oauth-tokens.dat` does not exist — regardless of which backend actually held the pair at call time: keyring-only, DPAPI-only (EC-1.4.038-1), or both incl. a stale/orphaned DPAPI file (EC-1.4.038-2). `remove_if_present` NEVER fails the overall clear on a missing file (`NotFound` is success, mirroring `delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry`), but a GENUINE filesystem error (permission denied, distinct from not-found) PROPAGATES rather than being swallowed (EC-1.4.038-3, extending BC-1.2.014's "genuine errors surfaced" contract to this third backend). **Ordering/fan-out (Postcondition 4):** for a mix of outcomes across the deletion set — e.g. a genuine keyring error on one step and a genuine (or tolerated-`NotFound`) outcome on another — EVERY step is still attempted; the call NEVER short-circuits on the first genuine error, so no backend is left with an undeleted remnant merely because an earlier step in the sequence failed (EC-1.4.038-4). The error ultimately returned to the caller is the FIRST genuine error encountered in the function's fixed attempt order, returned only AFTER all attempts complete. For `clear_profile_creds`, credential deletion (including the DPAPI file) precedes config-entry removal (BC-1.2.014 ordering, Postcondition 2), and this precedence holds independent of whether an earlier credential-deletion step failed. **Clear-path `ProfilePathEscape` tolerance — new oracle assertions (Pass-8 adversarial review Findings #3 and #2; Postcondition 4's `remove_if_present`-outcome-discrimination clause, Invariant 3, EC-1.4.038-5/6; ADR-0021 §7's `clear_dpapi_file_tolerating_path_escape` adapter):** (1) **Guard-colliding name clears successfully — cross-platform, DEFAULT CI, NO `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` seam.** Given a profile name that FAILS `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (cover at least a name containing `:` and a reserved device-name stem such as `con`), `clear_profile_oauth_pair(profile)` AND, separately, `clear_profile_creds(profile)` each return `Ok(())` — NOT `Err` downcastable to `ProfilePathEscape`, and not any other error — AND every keyring deletion step in the function's fixed attempt order is still observed to have been attempted (via the same fault-injection/spy seam the other BC-1.4.038 attempt-all assertions use). This runs in DEFAULT CI and needs NO `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` seam: it exercises the guard-rejection branch of `remove_if_present`, reachable identically on EVERY OS via `file_path`'s mandatory first-statement guard call on BOTH cfg arms (Pass-4 Finding #2), with no dependency on `TooLong` or DPAPI engagement — the clear-path caller-side adapter `clear_dpapi_file_tolerating_path_escape` maps `ProfilePathEscape`→`Ok(())` exactly as it maps `NotFound`, and the guard rejects at path-construction time before any keychain read/DPAPI touch, so no real-keychain STATE persistence is needed to observe the DPAPI-removal step's own `Ok(())` outcome. (2) **Tolerated-`ProfilePathEscape` vs. genuine-FS-error stay DISTINCT.** Inject a `ProfilePathEscape` from `remove_if_present` in one case (expect `Ok(())` — TOLERATED via the adapter) and a DIFFERENT, distinct genuine `anyhow` error (e.g. a simulated permission-denied) from `remove_if_present` in a sibling case (expect that error to be the function's PROPAGATED result, per EC-1.4.038-3): the two cases MUST DIVERGE, so a future change collapsing the two classifications back together fails THIS VP rather than silently passing. (3) **Reserved-device-name case passes on Windows too.** Include a case naming a profile a reserved Windows device-name stem (e.g. `"con"`) and assert the clear succeeds when the TEST SUITE ITSELF runs on Windows, not only on Linux/macOS — this property is NOT gated to non-Windows platforms, unlike `remove_if_present`'s own `#[cfg(not(windows))]` no-op arm. (4) **This VP asserts NOTHING about a successful DPAPI write.** It is purely a clear-path/guard-interaction property, fully decoupled from `TooLong`/DPAPI engagement — it does not assert that a new, successful `store_pair` ever occurred for the tested profile. **Three independently-failing `ProfilePathEscape` properties across the delta (Pass-8 Finding #2).** VP-AUTHDX-016 proves the guard EMITS `ProfilePathEscape` at `file_path`/the three entry points; VP-AUTHDX-017 proves the STORE path (Sites 1/3) RENDERS it as a distinct exit-64 invalid-profile-name error; VP-AUTHDX-018 (this VP) proves the CLEAR path SWALLOWS it → `Ok(())`. These are THREE genuinely different properties at three different call sites, each capable of failing independently if any one regresses — the clear path's ABSENCE of any rendered error (`Ok(())` returned, no message text to inspect) is a different and stronger claim than "the correct message was rendered," the shape VP-016/VP-017 assert. **Coverage boundary:** the deletion invocation + `NotFound`-tolerance + `ProfilePathEscape`-tolerance + genuine-error-propagation + attempt-all-before-returning logic is cross-platform-unit-testable by asserting `remove_if_present` (and every keyring delete) is called regardless of an earlier step's injected failure, and that the propagated error matches the first-in-order genuine failure; on macOS/Linux `remove_if_present` is an `Ok(())` no-op for a profile name that PASSES `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3; a guard-REJECTING name returns `Err(ProfilePathEscape)` on every OS instead — cross-ref VP-AUTHDX-016(d) / BC-1.4.036 EC-1.4.036-7), so the real file-deletion-and-absence assertion runs on a Windows CI runner or a keyring-gated integration test extending `tests/auth_remove_logout_semantics.rs`. **Verification method**: unit/integration test asserting all deletion steps are attempted regardless of injected earlier-step failures, `NotFound` is tolerated, a genuine FS/backend error propagates as the first-in-order failure, and no step is skipped (cross-platform for the invocation/error-fold logic); real DPAPI-file-absence-post-clear assertion on Windows CI / keyring-gated. **F6 target**: `src/api/auth.rs::clear_profile_oauth_pair`, `::clear_profile_creds`, `::clear_dpapi_file_tolerating_path_escape` (the caller-side adapter mapping `ProfilePathEscape`→`Ok(())`, ADR-0021 §7); `src/api/auth_windows_store.rs::remove_if_present`.

**Related BCs**: BC-1.2.013 (`auth logout` non-destructive contract this BC's `clear_profile_oauth_pair` step serves), BC-1.2.014 (`auth remove` four-step delete this BC's `clear_profile_creds` step serves), BC-1.4.035 (the storage side this BC's deletion mirrors), BC-1.4.037 (the file this BC deletes), BC-1.4.040 (source of the `reject_unsafe_profile_component` guard and the `ProfilePathEscape` marker this BC's clear path tolerates), BC-1.4.039 (the store/read-path sibling that, unlike this BC, DOES render `ProfilePathEscape` as a distinct user-visible error — see the asymmetry note above, Pass-8 adversarial review Finding #2), BC-6.1.004 (`validate_profile_name` — the PRIMARY, live gate that makes BC-1.4.040's guard, and by extension this BC's clear-path tolerance, defense-in-depth rather than a live-vulnerability fix; Pass-20 gate-audit correction), BC-6.1.005 (validation runs at config-load and the CLI-flag/resolved-active-profile-name boundary; Pass-20 gate-audit correction).

**Architecture Anchors**: ADR-0021 §7 (as amended, Pass-8 — the `clear_dpapi_file_tolerating_path_escape` adapter); architecture-delta.md §2.1 (module interface table), §3 (modified-components table), §16 (Pass-8 adversarial review amendments).

**Story Anchor**: TBD (F3)

**VP Anchors**: VP-AUTHDX-018

**Trace**: F2 spec evolution, cycle-004 `windows-correctness` (2026-09-03), issue #759, DEC-334; ADR-0021 §7; architecture-delta.md §2.1/§3; cross-reference BC-1.2.013/BC-1.2.014 (the logout/remove contracts this BC extends), `tests/auth_remove_logout_semantics.rs` (existing test suite to extend). Pass-8 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-03): Finding #3 (MED, backward-compat regression) — Postcondition 4 amended with the `remove_if_present`-outcome-discrimination clause (a `ProfilePathEscape` is TOLERATED via the new `clear_dpapi_file_tolerating_path_escape` adapter, never the "first genuine error"); new backward-compat Invariant 3; new EC-1.4.038-5/6. Finding #2 (MED, dependent) — new explicit sentence stating the clear path never renders `ProfilePathEscape` as a user-visible error, unlike BC-1.4.036/BC-1.4.039 Sites 1/3, cross-referencing BC-1.4.039's Postcondition 1. Resolved by the architect via ADR-0021 §7 (as amended) and `architecture-delta.md` §16; no `src/` change. Pass-8 adversarial review VP follow-on (formal-verifier, 2026-09-03): VP-AUTHDX-018 gained the clear-path `ProfilePathEscape`-tolerance oracle assertions the architect's Pass-8 guidance specified (Finding #3: guard-colliding name clears to `Ok(())` with every keyring step still attempted, cross-platform DEFAULT CI, NO `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` seam — the guard rejects before any keychain/DPAPI touch; tolerated-`ProfilePathEscape` vs. genuine-FS-error kept provably distinct per EC-1.4.038-3; reserved-device-name case asserted to pass on Windows too; and an explicit statement that VP-016/017/018 are three independently-failing EMITS/RENDERS/SWALLOWS properties per Finding #2). No VP added/removed (cumulative VP count stays 55); VP-018 F6 target extended to name `clear_dpapi_file_tolerating_path_escape`. Companion record: `.factory/cycles/cycle-004/phase-f2-spec-evolution/vp-delta.md`. Pass-9 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-04): Finding #1 (MED) — Invariant 2's blanket "returns Ok(()) immediately … a permanent no-op" claim for macOS/Linux was overstated (same class as the BC-1.4.036 correction above): reworded to qualify the no-op as holding only for a profile name that PASSES `reject_unsafe_profile_component` — a guard-rejecting name returns `Err(ProfilePathEscape)` on every OS, which Postcondition 4's `clear_dpapi_file_tolerating_path_escape` adapter and Invariant 3/EC-1.4.038-5 already depend on being reachable. No postcondition/invariant renumbering; no VP change. Pass-20 / gate-audit correction (2026-09-04, ADR-0021 §7): Pass-8's original "pre-existing, working profile named `con`" backward-compat premise for Invariant 3/EC-1.4.038-5 is FALSE for any config `jr` has ever loaded — `validate_profile_name` (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005) already rejects such a name at config-load, before any `auth` subcommand dispatches. Invariant 3 and EC-1.4.038-5 reworded to state the corrected justification: the guarantee is defense-in-depth against (a) a future relaxation of `validate_profile_name`'s charset/reserved-name rules or (b) a validation-call-site regression bypassing that gate — not a fix for a reachable pre-existing profile. New "Related BCs" cross-reference to BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005. **No postcondition, guard, or adapter behavior changed** — `clear_dpapi_file_tolerating_path_escape` and `remove_if_present` are unmodified; framing/cross-refs only. No `#### BC-` heading added or removed; total_bcs (80)/definitional_count (69) UNCHANGED. VP-AUTHDX-018 untouched — formal-verifier confirmed no oracle text there asserts the false premise (architect's Pass-20 guidance item 4).
