#### BC-1.4.039: Honest-fail backstop — `DpapiFallbackFailed` replaces "Unlock your keychain" only when the DPAPI fallback itself fails

**Confidence**: HIGH
**Source**: ADR-0021 §6; `src/api/auth.rs::oauth_login`, `::refresh_oauth_token_with_url` (F4 targets)
**Subject**: Auth & Identity
**Origin**: NEW (cycle-004 `windows-correctness`, DEC-334, ADR-0021 §6; supersedes the misdirecting message this cycle's root-cause analysis identifies, F1 §4)

**Description**: A new, type-based (not string-matched) marker error `DpapiFallbackFailed(String)` distinguishes "the DPAPI fallback itself failed" (disk full, DPAPI syscall failure, permission denied) from every other error shape. Two of the four existing "Unlock your keychain" message sites in `src/api/auth.rs` are revised to branch on this marker; the other two are confirmed unaffected. **The two revised sites do NOT share one verbatim honest-fail message (Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #3):** Site 1 is a LOGIN (the OAuth grant this failure concerns was just created by the same failed attempt and is genuinely unused) while Site 3 is a REFRESH of an EXISTING, currently-in-use OAuth session (the grant may still back other active credential material for this profile) — a single shared message that instructs the user to "revoke the now-unused Atlassian grant" is factually correct at Site 1 and actively harmful advice at Site 3, where the grant is in active use and revoking it would destroy the user's working auth rather than clean up an unused one. Each site therefore gets its own message text, worded for its own scenario.

**Preconditions**:
1. `store_oauth_tokens` (BC-1.4.035) has already attempted the keyring-first write and, on `TooLong`, attempted the DPAPI fallback (BC-1.4.037), and that fallback itself returned an error.

**Postconditions**:
1. **Site 1** (`oauth_login`'s store-failure `map_err`, a LOGIN) and **Site 3** (`refresh_oauth_token_with_url`'s post-refresh store-failure `map_err`, a REFRESH): both branch, in order, FIRST on `e.downcast_ref::<ProfilePathEscape>()`, then — only if that did not match — on `e.downcast_ref::<DpapiFallbackFailed>()` (Pass-5 adversarial review, Finding #2; ADR-0021 §6 as amended):
   - **`ProfilePathEscape` `Some(_)` → checked FIRST at BOTH sites, BEFORE the `DpapiFallbackFailed` check below.** The SAME distinct exit-64 "invalid profile name" `JrError::UserError` as the read path (BC-1.4.036) — naming the invalid profile — is propagated: never the `DpapiFallbackFailed` honest-fail message, and never the generic "Unlock your keychain" fallback. A bad profile name must never render as either of those. **This case can only arise here despite `validate_profile_name` (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005) already having validated the profile name at config-load/CLI-flag boundary before any subcommand dispatches (Pass-20 gate-audit correction, 2026-09-04, ADR-0021 §9) — i.e. only via a future relaxation of that validation's charset/reserved-name rules, or a validation-call-site regression bypassing it entirely, later failing `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (BC-1.4.040) inside `store_pair`'s guard call**; it is included for completeness and symmetry with the read path, not because it is expected to fire often in practice.
   - **`DpapiFallbackFailed` `Some(_)` (only reached if `ProfilePathEscape` did not match) — the two sites select DISTINCT honest-fail message text (Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #3) — they must NOT share one verbatim template**, because the underlying Atlassian OAuth grant's state differs materially between a fresh login and a refresh of an already-established session:
     - **Site 1, `Some(_)` (login scenario)** → `"Authorization succeeded with Atlassian, but the OAuth tokens were too large for Windows Credential Manager's 2560-byte limit AND jr's encrypted-file fallback also failed ({inner}). Check available disk space and file permissions, then run \"jr auth login --oauth --profile {profile}\" again. You must first revoke the now-unused Atlassian grant: visit https://id.atlassian.com/manage-profile/apps."` — the grant-revoke step is stated as a REQUIRED action, not an aside, because this specific grant was created by the SAME failed attempt, was never successfully persisted as a working credential, and has no other consumer — revoking it is genuinely safe cleanup.
     - **Site 3, `Some(_)` (refresh scenario)** → `"The OAuth refresh succeeded with Atlassian, but the new tokens were too large for Windows Credential Manager's 2560-byte limit AND jr's encrypted-file fallback also failed ({inner}). Check available disk space and file permissions, then run \"jr auth login --oauth --profile {profile}\" again to re-authenticate."` — this message MUST NOT instruct the user to revoke any Atlassian grant: the refresh token consumed by this exchange is already dead server-side (single-use, EC-1.4.039-4), but the GRANT itself may still be backing other active sessions or refresh chains for this profile, and revoking it would destroy working auth rather than clean up something unused.
   - **`None` (neither marker matched, either site) → the existing "Unlock your keychain (or grant access to jr)…" message is UNCHANGED** — it remains accurate for every error that reaches this branch with neither a `ProfilePathEscape` nor a `DpapiFallbackFailed` marker: a genuine lock/permission condition on the small-secret keyring path, OR (on macOS/Linux, ALWAYS — see Invariant 3) a backend error where DPAPI was never engaged because `engage_dpapi_fallback` (BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3) is `#[cfg(not(windows))]`-hardcoded to `false`.
2. **Site 2** (`refresh_oauth_token_with_url`'s `load_oauth_tokens` read-failure branch): NO message-text change — this site becomes DPAPI-aware transitively via the corrected `load_oauth_tokens` (BC-1.4.036), which itself distinguishes a genuine backend error (unchanged message here) from a corrupt-DPAPI-file condition (its own distinct message, surfaced before this branch is ever reached).
3. **Site 4** (`resolve_refresh_app_credentials`'s BYO app-credential-read error): UNCHANGED. This site guards the OAuth APP's `client_id`/`client_secret` pair — always short strings, never `TooLong`-reachable. F4 must AUDIT (not modify) this site to confirm no `TooLong` path is reachable here.
4. **Site-3-only proactive stale-pair clear (Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #7, resolved at F2 — see EC-1.4.039-4).** When Site 3's `DpapiFallbackFailed` branch fires (Postcondition 1's Site-3 `Some(_)` arm), `refresh_oauth_token_with_url` ADDITIONALLY clears the profile's now-stale stored OAuth pair — via the same deletion step BC-1.4.038's `clear_profile_oauth_pair` already provides — as part of returning the honest-fail error, before returning to the caller. This postcondition is Site-3-SPECIFIC: it does not apply to Site 1, which has no meaningfully "stale" prior pair in play for a brand-new login failure. **This clear fires wherever Site 3 (`refresh_oauth_token_with_url`) is reached, not only from an explicit `jr auth refresh` invocation — it fires identically on the transparent, mid-command 401-reconcile refresh path (`JiraClient::send` invoking `refresh_oauth_token_with_url` when a request receives a 401 mid-command; Pass-3 adversarial review observation).** This is acceptable and needs no different handling for that caller: the refresh token consumed on either path is already server-side-consumed by the time this branch fires (EC-1.4.039-4), so the clear's rationale — the local pair is a known-dead artifact, not a still-pending replacement — applies identically regardless of whether an explicit `auth refresh` or a transparent mid-command reconcile triggered the refresh. Rationale: the refresh token this Site-3 refresh attempt consumed is single-use and is already invalidated server-side the instant Atlassian's refresh response was received (CLAUDE.md, "Refresh tokens are strictly single-use on Atlassian"), independent of whether the subsequent local store succeeds — so leaving the old pair in place guarantees a confusing `invalid_grant` on the profile's next use with no diagnostic connecting it back to this failure, whereas proactively clearing it makes the next command observe a clean "No stored OAuth token" state, consistent with this message's own instruction to re-run `jr auth login --oauth --profile {profile}`. **This does NOT conflict with cycle-003's "never clear before confirmed replacement" invariant (DEC-321, relogin-then-replace, `src/cli/auth/refresh.rs::refresh_credentials`):** DEC-321 governs the ordinary case where a replacement credential MIGHT still be obtained and stored via a different attempt, and requires resolving the new value BEFORE clearing the old one so a failed attempt never leaves the profile worse off than before it started. By the time Site 3's `DpapiFallbackFailed` branch is reached, BOTH the Atlassian-side exchange (irreversibly succeeded, consuming the old refresh token) AND the local store attempt for the new pair (irrecoverably failed on both keyring and the DPAPI fallback) have already run to completion — there is no pending "confirmed replacement" left to wait for within this call, and the OLD local value is already semantically dead. Clearing it here is cleanup of a known-dead artifact, not clearing-before-replacement of a credential that might still turn out to be good. **Reconciliation with BC-1.4.035's delete-first store ordering — RETAIN as explicit defense-in-depth, do NOT remove (Pass-7 adversarial review Finding #2).** BC-1.4.035 Postconditions 2/3/6 (Pass-3 adversarial review Finding #1) already have `store_oauth_tokens` delete the profile's ENTIRE existing keyring pair BEFORE attempting `auth_windows_store::store_pair` on either `TooLong` arm; `DpapiFallbackFailed` is produced only when that SAME `store_pair` attempt then itself fails — i.e., strictly AFTER the keyring delete has already run. Consequently, by the time this Site-3 clear fires, the keyring pair is typically already gone (the prior delete-first step removed it) and no DPAPI file was ever successfully written (that is precisely why `DpapiFallbackFailed` fired) — so in the common case this Site-3 `clear_profile_oauth_pair` call is REDUNDANT with work `store_oauth_tokens` already did. This redundancy is INTENTIONAL and the clear MUST NOT be "optimized away" as dead code: (1) `clear_profile_oauth_pair`/`delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry` are NotFound-tolerant by construction (BC-1.4.038), so re-deleting an already-absent pair is a harmless no-op, not an error; (2) this Site-3 call is deliberately retained to cover any FUTURE code path that could produce a `DpapiFallbackFailed` marker without the delete-first step having already executed (e.g. a future refactor of the write path, or a different producer of the marker) — removing this clear on the grounds that "the delete-first step already handles it" would silently reintroduce the stale-pair risk EC-1.4.039-4 exists to close, for any such future path. An F4 implementer must keep this call exactly as specified, not treat its current redundancy as license to drop it.

**Invariants**:
1. The honest-fail message is reachable ONLY when BOTH keyring AND the DPAPI store have failed for a given write — with the DPAPI fallback in place (BC-1.4.035/BC-1.4.037), this is now a true edge case, not the common path #759 originally reported.
2. Every "Unlock your keychain" message site is accurate for the failure it actually reports, post this BC — no site says "locked keychain" for a `TooLong`-originated failure once the DPAPI fallback exists.
3. **In RELEASE (production) builds, the `DpapiFallbackFailed`-branch message is UNREACHABLE on macOS/Linux BY CONSTRUCTION, not merely "not currently triggered there" (Pass-1 adversarial review Finding #5).** `DpapiFallbackFailed` can only ever be produced by `auth_windows_store::store_pair` failing (BC-1.4.035 Postcondition 5), and `store_pair` is only ever called after `engage_dpapi_fallback` returns `true` (BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3) — a function whose `#[cfg(not(windows))]` arm is, in a RELEASE build, hardcoded `false` regardless of the underlying `keyring::Error`. On a non-Windows RELEASE compilation there is therefore no code path, at any call site, by which `e.downcast_ref::<DpapiFallbackFailed>()` can ever observe `Some(_)`: the `Some(_)` arm's honest-fail message is dead code on macOS/Linux release builds in the same structural sense that `auth_windows_store`'s DPAPI FFI functions do not exist there at all (VP-AUTHDX-013). **Pass-6 adversarial review, Finding #1 (BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3; ADR-0021 §1): this unreachability is DELIBERATELY, and only, lifted in a `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` build with the `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` test seam set** — under that seam, for the triggering `keyring::Error::TooLong` (the only variant that ever reaches this call site — BC-1.4.035 Postconditions 2/3), `engage_dpapi_fallback` returns `should_fallback_to_dpapi(err)`, i.e. `true`; `store_pair` is invoked, and on a non-Windows debug build `store_pair`'s `#[cfg(not(windows))]` arm returns `DpapiFallbackFailed` immediately (BC-1.4.037 Precondition 2), so the `Some(_)` arm and its honest-fail message ARE reachable and exercised in default CI under the seam — this is exactly the mechanism VP-AUTHDX-011/012/022 depend on. **Corrected by Pass-7 adversarial review, Finding #1 (ADR-0021 §1's authoritative code):** the seam does NOT make `engage_dpapi_fallback` return `true` regardless of the error variant — a non-`TooLong` `Err` under the seam still returns `false` from `should_fallback_to_dpapi` and propagates unchanged, exactly as it does without the seam (BC-1.4.035 Postcondition 4/Invariant 3); the seam only lifts the `#[cfg(not(windows))]` hardcoded-`false` so the `TooLong` predicate can be evaluated on non-Windows in the first place. This is a compile-time/call-site-gating guarantee about RELEASE builds (and debug builds with the seam unset) specifically, testable directly (feed a mocked `TooLong` into `store_oauth_tokens` on a non-Windows test run with the seam unset and assert the legacy message, never the honest-fail one, per BC-1.4.035's own Invariant 3 test guidance) — not a probabilistic claim resting on DPAPI syscalls merely being unlikely to fail on platforms that don't have them.
4. **`ProfilePathEscape` is checked FIRST at Sites 1 and 3, before `DpapiFallbackFailed` and before the generic keychain-lock fallback (Pass-5 adversarial review, Finding #2; ADR-0021 §6 as amended).** A bad profile name must never render as the honest-fail message (Postcondition 1's `DpapiFallbackFailed` `Some(_)` arm) and must never render as "Unlock your keychain" (Postcondition 1's `None` arm) — it renders ONLY as the distinct exit-64 invalid-profile-name error, identical to the read path's discrimination order (BC-1.4.036 Invariants 1/3). This mirrors the read path's ordering rationale exactly: `ProfilePathEscape` signals a problem with the CALLER's input (the profile name), not with the credential or the storage backend, so conflating it with either of the other two messages would misdirect the user toward "check disk space and revoke the grant" or "unlock your keychain" when the actual fix is "use a different profile name."

**Edge Cases**:
- EC-1.4.039-1: `store_oauth_tokens` fails with a genuine lock/permission error on the SMALL-secret keyring path (never reaches `TooLong`, DPAPI never engaged) → Sites 1/3 see `None` from the downcast and the existing "Unlock your keychain" message fires correctly — this is the majority case on macOS/Linux and for any Windows secret that fits. On macOS/Linux specifically, in a RELEASE build this is not merely the majority case but the ONLY reachable case (Invariant 3): `engage_dpapi_fallback` is hardcoded `false` there, so `TooLong` itself is matched and propagated exactly as before this cycle, never routing toward the honest-fail message. (Pass-6 adversarial review, Finding #1: in a `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` build with `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` set, this is no longer the only reachable case — see Invariant 3's debug-seam exception.)
- EC-1.4.039-2: The DPAPI fallback's file write fails for a reason OTHER than disk-full/permission (e.g., a transient DPAPI syscall failure) → the `{inner}` interpolation in the honest-fail message carries whatever diagnostic string the underlying `anyhow::Error` provides; this BC does not enumerate every possible `{inner}` value, only the message TEMPLATE and trigger condition.
- EC-1.4.039-3: A future refactor accidentally allows `TooLong` to reach Site 4 (the BYO app-credential path) — this BC's Postcondition 3 requires this to be AUDITED at F4 (client_id/client_secret are short strings by construction, so this is a confirmation task, not an anticipated real failure mode); if it did occur, Site 4 remains unchanged and would misreport a `TooLong` as a lock/permission issue — flagged here as an out-of-scope residual for F4 to confirm is unreachable, not fixed by this BC.
- EC-1.4.039-4: **Refresh-path store-failure data loss (Pass-1 adversarial review Finding #8).** A token REFRESH succeeds with Atlassian — the single-use refresh token presented in the request is now CONSUMED server-side and cannot be reused (see CLAUDE.md "Refresh tokens are strictly single-use on Atlassian") — but the SUBSEQUENT `store_oauth_tokens` call at Site 3 fails with `DpapiFallbackFailed` (both keyring AND the DPAPI fallback failed to persist the new pair). The profile is now left holding the OLD, already-consumed refresh token on disk/keyring (never cleared, since `store_oauth_tokens`'s write attempt failing does not itself delete the pre-existing stored pair) while the NEW access+refresh pair returned by Atlassian is nowhere persisted — the next command that attempts to use or refresh this profile's credentials will present the stale, already-invalidated refresh token and receive `invalid_grant` from Atlassian, a confusing failure mode that does not explain WHY the token is invalid (a well-behaved refresh token simply expired would look identical to the user). This BC's Site 3 honest-fail message (Postcondition 1, `Some(_)` arm) IS the mechanism that covers this exact scenario — it fires at Site 3, the refresh site, not only at Site 1 (initial `oauth_login`) — and its instruction to run `jr auth login --oauth --profile {profile}` again is the correct remediation (a fresh login, not a refresh, is required once the refresh token is consumed). **Resolved at F2 (Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #7): option (a).** The now-invalid stored pair IS proactively cleared at Site 3 as part of the `DpapiFallbackFailed` honest-fail path — see the new Postcondition 4 for the mechanism. Leaving the stale pair in place (option (b)) was rejected: it guarantees a confusing `invalid_grant` on the profile's next attempted use with no diagnostic connecting it back to this failure, whereas the server-side credential (the consumed refresh token) is already dead by this point regardless of which option is chosen, so clearing the local copy discards nothing still usable — it is pure cleanup. Postcondition 4 also reconciles this proactive clear with cycle-003's "never clear before confirmed replacement" invariant (DEC-321, relogin-then-replace): that invariant governs attempts where a replacement might still be obtained, which does not describe this branch, since both the Atlassian-side exchange and the local store attempt have already irrecoverably run to completion by the time this clear fires.
- EC-1.4.039-5 (Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #2; reclassified Pass-20 gate-audit correction, 2026-09-04): Site 1's or Site 3's store error contains a `ProfilePathEscape` marker (the profile name itself fails `reject_unsafe_profile_component`, BC-1.4.040) — checked FIRST, before `DpapiFallbackFailed` and before the generic "Unlock your keychain" fallback → the SAME distinct exit-64 invalid-profile-name error as the read path (BC-1.4.036) fires at both sites; this case can only arise if a profile name that already passed `validate_profile_name`'s (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005) config-load/CLI-flag validation later fails the guard inside `store_pair`'s call — reachable only via a future relaxation of that validation's charset/reserved-name rules or a validation-call-site regression bypassing it (Pass-20 gate-audit correction, ADR-0021 §9) — included for completeness and symmetry with the read path rather than as an expected common occurrence.

**Verification Properties**:
- **VP-AUTHDX-017 — Honest-fail message selection: `DpapiFallbackFailed` vs. ordinary keyring error; UNREACHABLE on macOS/Linux by construction (SAFETY INVARIANT, cross-platform, NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 VP-delta pass; non-Windows unreachability strengthened from "unlikely" to "structural" per Pass-1 adversarial review Finding #5).** Property: at the two revised message sites (Site 1 `oauth_login`'s store-failure `map_err`; Site 3 `refresh_oauth_token_with_url`'s post-refresh store-failure `map_err`), given a store error whose chain contains a `DpapiFallbackFailed` marker, `e.downcast_ref::<DpapiFallbackFailed>()` is `Some(_)` and the NEW honest-fail message is selected — both sites name the 2560-byte Credential Manager limit and the encrypted-file-fallback failure (`{inner}`), but the two sites select DISTINCT text and MUST NOT share one verbatim template (Postcondition 1, `Some(_)` arm, Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #3): **Site 1 (a LOGIN)** additionally instructs the user to revoke the now-unused Atlassian grant (created by this same failed attempt, no other consumer — safe cleanup), whereas the **Site 3 (a REFRESH)** message MUST NOT contain any grant-revoke instruction — the oracle asserts its ABSENCE at Site 3, since the grant may still back other active sessions for the profile and revoking it would destroy working auth (Postcondition 1's Site-3 `Some(_)` arm); given ANY store error WITHOUT that marker (a genuine lock/permission `keyring::Error` on the small-secret path, or any non-Windows backend error where DPAPI was never engaged), the downcast is `None` and the UNCHANGED "Unlock your keychain…" message is selected (Postcondition 1, `None` arm; EC-1.4.039-1). **`ProfilePathEscape` RENDERING, checked FIRST at both sites (Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #2, Postcondition 1's `ProfilePathEscape` arm, Invariant 4, EC-1.4.039-5):** given a store error whose chain contains a `ProfilePathEscape` marker (the profile name itself failed `reject_unsafe_profile_component`, BC-1.4.040), `e.downcast_ref::<ProfilePathEscape>()` is `Some(_)` and BOTH Site 1 and Site 3 render the SAME distinct exit-64 invalid-profile-name `JrError::UserError` naming the profile — discriminated BEFORE the `DpapiFallbackFailed` honest-fail message AND before the generic "Unlock your keychain" fallback — NEVER either of those two. The oracle constructs a `ProfilePathEscape`-wrapped store error and asserts the invalid-profile-name RENDERING at each site, text-distinct from both the honest-fail and the keychain-lock messages; this is a DISTINCT property from VP-AUTHDX-016 — 016 proves the guard EMITS `ProfilePathEscape`, whereas THIS asserts Sites 1/3 RENDER it correctly and first, so the two fail independently if either regresses. The honest-fail message is reachable ONLY when BOTH keyring AND the DPAPI store have failed for a given write (Invariant 1); on macOS/Linux specifically, it is UNREACHABLE BY CONSTRUCTION (Invariant 3) — `engage_dpapi_fallback`'s `#[cfg(not(windows))]` hardcoded `false` means `store_pair` (the only producer of `DpapiFallbackFailed`) is never called there, so the `Some(_)` arm is dead code on non-Windows, not merely unobserved in testing. **Site-3 proactive stale-pair clear (Postcondition 4, Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #7; NotFound-TOLERANT defense-in-depth per Pass-7 adversarial review Finding #2):** additionally, when Site 3's `Some(_)` arm fires, `refresh_oauth_token_with_url` MUST clear the profile's stored OAuth pair via `clear_profile_oauth_pair` (BC-1.4.038) before returning the honest-fail error. This clear is, since Pass-3 Finding #1's delete-first `store_oauth_tokens` ordering (BC-1.4.035 Postconditions 2/3/6), TYPICALLY REDUNDANT by the time `DpapiFallbackFailed` fires — the keyring pair is usually ALREADY gone (the delete-first step removed it) and no DPAPI file was ever written — so the oracle does NOT require the clear to find any pair present; it requires ONLY that the clear RUNS, TOLERATES ABSENCE (its `NotFound`-tolerant keyring deletes hitting an already-absent key is the EXPECTED common case and MUST NOT surface as an error — BC-1.4.038's `delete_credential_tolerating_no_entry` contract), and LEAVES NO stored pair. The oracle asserts that after a Site-3 `DpapiFallbackFailed`, a subsequent `load_oauth_tokens` for that profile observes NO stored pair (a clean "no stored OAuth token" state) REGARDLESS of whether a pair was present when the clear ran — matching the defense-in-depth framing — NOT the stale, already-server-side-consumed pair that would otherwise surface as a confusing `invalid_grant` on next use (EC-1.4.039-4). The clear is RETAINED intentionally as this NotFound-tolerant defense-in-depth covering any future code path that could produce `DpapiFallbackFailed` without the delete-first step having run, and MUST NOT be optimized away as apparent dead code (Postcondition 4). This proactive clear is Site-3-ONLY (the oracle asserts Site 1 does NOT clear, having no meaningfully stale prior pair for a brand-new login failure), and does NOT violate cycle-003's "never clear before confirmed replacement" invariant (DEC-321) because both the Atlassian exchange and the local store attempt have already irrecoverably completed — the cleared pair is a known-dead artifact, not a still-pending replacement. Site 2 is unchanged (becomes DPAPI-aware transitively via the corrected `load_oauth_tokens`, VP-AUTHDX-015); Site 4 (`resolve_refresh_app_credentials`) is UNCHANGED and guards short app-credential strings never `TooLong`-reachable (Postcondition 3 — an F4 audit-only confirmation, EC-1.4.039-3). **Verification method**: unit test constructing a `DpapiFallbackFailed`-wrapped error vs. an ordinary `keyring::Error` vs. a `ProfilePathEscape`-wrapped error and asserting the message text selected at Sites 1/3 — including that Site 1 CONTAINS and Site 3 OMITS the grant-revoke instruction, and that a `ProfilePathEscape`-marked error RENDERS FIRST as the exit-64 invalid-profile-name message at both sites (never honest-fail, never "Unlock your keychain"; Finding #2) — plus, for a Site-3 `DpapiFallbackFailed`, asserting via a seam over `clear_profile_oauth_pair` / `load_oauth_tokens`-returns-`Ok(None)` that the profile's stored pair is cleared afterward (and that Site 1 clears nothing); fully cross-platform via constructed error values, no real Windows/DPAPI dependency, runs in default CI; the non-Windows unreachability claim is additionally provable by feeding a mocked `TooLong` into `store_oauth_tokens` on a non-Windows test run WITH `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` UNSET and asserting the LEGACY message (never honest-fail) appears, per BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3's own test guidance. **Test-serialization requirement (Pass-6 adversarial review Finding #4).** That env-UNSET legacy-message assertion is the direct counterpart of the seam-engaged (`=1`) delete-then-fail tests (VP-AUTHDX-011/012/022): both observe the SAME process-global `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK` at the SAME `engage_dpapi_fallback` call site and assert OPPOSING outcomes, so this env-UNSET test MUST participate in the SAME `env_lock`-style `std::sync::Mutex` those VPs require (the `JR_SERVICE_NAME` pattern in `tests/oauth_refresh_integration.rs`, NOT `--test-threads=1`) — otherwise `cargo test`'s default parallelism can interleave a `set_var`/`remove_var` from one class with an in-flight assertion in the other. **F6 target**: `src/api/auth.rs::oauth_login`, `::refresh_oauth_token_with_url` (the two `map_err` message-selection branches, plus Site 3's proactive stale-pair clear); `src/api/auth_windows_store.rs::DpapiFallbackFailed`; `src/api/auth.rs::engage_dpapi_fallback` (the call-site gate making non-Windows unreachability structural); `src/api/auth.rs::clear_profile_oauth_pair` (the Site-3 clear-on-honest-fail step, Postcondition 4).

**Related BCs**: BC-1.4.035 (the routing/rollback logic whose failure this BC's marker error signals), BC-1.4.037 (the DPAPI store whose failure produces the marker), BC-1.4.036 (Site 2's DPAPI-awareness, achieved transitively — VP-AUTHDX-015), BC-1.4.030 (Site 4's function — `resolve_refresh_app_credentials` — confirmed UNCHANGED/unaffected by this BC), BC-1.4.040 (source of the `ProfilePathEscape` marker Sites 1/3 now discriminate FIRST, before `DpapiFallbackFailed`, Pass-5 adversarial review Finding #2), BC-6.1.004 (`validate_profile_name` — the PRIMARY, live gate that makes this BC's `ProfilePathEscape` branch defense-in-depth-only; Pass-20 gate-audit correction), BC-6.1.005 (validation runs at config-load and the CLI-flag/resolved-active-profile-name boundary; Pass-20 gate-audit correction).

**Architecture Anchors**: ADR-0021 §6; architecture-delta.md §2.1 (module interface table — `DpapiFallbackFailed` row), §3 (modified-components table, Sites 1/3 rows).

**Story Anchor**: TBD (F3)

**VP Anchors**: VP-AUTHDX-017

**Trace**: F2 spec evolution, cycle-004 `windows-correctness` (2026-09-03), issue #759, DEC-334; ADR-0021 §6; architecture-delta.md §2.1/§3; F1 delta analysis §4 (root-cause BC), §5.2 (four message sites); cross-reference BC-1.4.030 (Site 4, confirmed unaffected). Pass-5 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-03): Finding #2 (MED) — Postcondition 1 (Sites 1/3, store-error rendering) reworded so the `map_err` discrimination checks `ProfilePathEscape` FIRST — before `DpapiFallbackFailed`'s honest-fail message and before the generic "Unlock your keychain" fallback — per ADR-0021 §6 as amended, so a bad profile name never renders as either; new Invariant 4; new EC-1.4.039-5. Pass-6 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-03): Finding #1 (HIGH) — Invariant 3 and EC-1.4.039-1 reworded to scope the "unreachable on macOS/Linux by construction" claim to RELEASE builds specifically, and to state the `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`-gated `JR_FORCE_DPAPI_FALLBACK=1` test seam (ADR-0021 §1, cross-referenced via BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3) under which this branch is deliberately reachable in default CI for VP-AUTHDX-011/012/022 — closing the same class of contradiction as BC-1.4.035's Finding #1. Pass-7 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-03): Finding #1 (MED) — Invariant 3's seam description corrected to state `engage_dpapi_fallback` returns `should_fallback_to_dpapi(err)` (true IFF the triggering error is `TooLong`) under the seam, not `true` regardless of the error variant, matching BC-1.4.035 Invariant 3's Pass-7 correction and ADR-0021 §1's authoritative code. Finding #2 (LOW) — Postcondition 4 gains an explicit reconciliation note: the Site-3 proactive clear is typically redundant with BC-1.4.035's Pass-3 delete-first `store_oauth_tokens` ordering (the keyring pair is usually already gone by the time `DpapiFallbackFailed` fires), and is RETAINED intentionally as NotFound-tolerant defense-in-depth covering any future code path that could produce the marker without the delete-first step having run — an F4 implementer must not remove it as apparent dead code. Pass-20 / gate-audit correction (2026-09-04, ADR-0021 §9): Postcondition 1's `ProfilePathEscape` bullet and EC-1.4.039-5 reworded to remove the false "there is none today"/"no guard exists at profile-creation time" premise — profile names ARE validated today by `validate_profile_name` (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005) at config-load/CLI-flag boundary; this branch is reachable only via a future relaxation of that validation or a validation-call-site regression bypassing it. #### BC-1.4.040: The DPAPI secret file's path is derived from the profile name with path-traversal / invalid-component rejection before any write, read, or delete

**Confidence**: MEDIUM (defense-in-depth hardening at the DPAPI secret-file path-join point — see Description; reclassified from "new hardening requirement closing a previously-undocumented risk" by the Pass-20 gate-audit correction, 2026-09-04, ADR-0021 §9)
**Source**: ADR-0021 §9 (`reject_unsafe_profile_component`, the concrete host-independent recognizer mandated by Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #1; reclassified as defense-in-depth by the Pass-20 gate-audit correction); architecture-delta.md §9 item 1 (residual design question, flagged by architect) and §21 (Pass-20 / gate-audit adversarial review amendments); `src/profile.rs` (`Profile::from(String)`, "Infallible by design," ADR-0011); `src/config.rs::validate_profile_name` (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005 — the PRIMARY, live profile-name validation gate this guard sits behind); `src/cache.rs::cache_dir` (the pre-existing, unsanitized precedent this BC's file_path function otherwise mirrors)
**Subject**: Auth & Identity
**Origin**: NEW (cycle-004 `windows-correctness`, DEC-334) — a defense-in-depth addition at the specific point (`file_path`) where a profile name is joined into a filesystem path, **not a closure of a live gap (Pass-20 gate-audit correction, 2026-09-04, ADR-0021 §9): a profile name reaching this point via any normal CLI/config path has ALREADY been validated by `validate_profile_name` (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005) at config-load (both strict and lenient loaders) and at the resolved-active-profile-name/CLI-flag boundary — restricting every such name to ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` ≤64 chars with the classic reserved-Windows-name stems excluded, a strict superset of the vectors this guard rejects — making every one of this guard's 30 guarded vectors already unreachable via any normal CLI/config path.** This guard instead protects against two future-hypothetical scenarios: (a) a future relaxation of `validate_profile_name`'s charset/reserved-name list, and (b) a validation-call-site regression that lets a name bypass BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005 entirely. `cache_dir(profile)` (`src/cache.rs`) already joins the raw profile string into a filesystem path with no sanitization of its own, and this ADR's new `auth_windows_store::file_path` was originally going to follow that identical convention (ADR-0021 §3) rather than invent a new rule. This BC ELEVATES that inherited pattern to an explicit, must-implement defense-in-depth requirement for the SECRET file specifically, because a secret file warrants an independent, cheap check at its own path-join point even though BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005 already cover the normal path.

**Description**: `Profile::from(String)` performs no validation of its own (ADR-0011, "Infallible by design") — a profile name can be any string, including one containing path-traversal-unsafe components (`..`, absolute-path-like segments, embedded path separators). **This does not mean a profile name reaches this guard unvalidated in practice (Pass-20 gate-audit correction, 2026-09-04, ADR-0021 §9): `validate_profile_name` (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005, `src/config.rs`) already restricts every profile name that can reach ANY credential-storage call site — this one included — to ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` ≤64 chars with reserved-Windows-name stems excluded, enforced at config-load and at the CLI-flag/resolved-active-profile-name boundary, BEFORE any subcommand (including any credential-storage call) ever dispatches.** Before this cycle, an equivalent join for the (disposable, non-secret) cache directory carried no sanitization of its own either and was treated as an accepted, low-priority residual (profile names are operator-controlled local config, not remote-attacker-controlled input). Because the new artifact governed by this BC is a SECRET — the OAuth token pair, not a disposable cache entry — `auth_windows_store::file_path(profile)` gains a mandatory guard, `reject_unsafe_profile_component(profile: &str) -> Result<(), ProfilePathEscape>`, invoked as `file_path`'s FIRST statement before the path is even joined; `file_path` is itself the SOLE call site of the recognizer and is in turn the shared function all three of `store_pair`/`load_pair`/`remove_if_present` call before any directory creation, read, write, or delete (Postcondition 8) — one implementation, not three independently-drifting checks. **As reclassified by the Pass-20 gate-audit correction, this guard is DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH against (a) a future relaxation of `validate_profile_name`'s charset/reserved-name list and (b) a validation-call-site regression bypassing BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005 — not a live CWE-22 closure; see BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005 for the primary, live gate.**

**This guard is a dedicated, host-independent, character-level recognizer — explicitly NOT `std::path::Path`/`Component` (Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #1, ADR-0021 §9).** `std::path`'s parsing behavior depends on the OS the check is COMPILED/RUN on: `Path::new("C:\\evil")` on a Linux CI runner yields a single opaque `Component::Normal("C:\\evil")` (backslash is not a Unix separator), which is lexically CONTAINED under `secrets/` and would be wrongly ACCEPTED — the exact opposite of the guard's intent — for `"C:\\evil"`, `"\\\\server\\share"` (UNC), and `"name:$DATA"` (NTFS Alternate Data Stream) alike. A `std::path`-based implementation would therefore pass a shallow read of "Windows-syntax-aware" while silently failing to actually recognize Windows path syntax whenever the check happens to run on Linux/macOS CI — falsifying the "runs in default CI, cross-platform" claim this guard depends on. `reject_unsafe_profile_component` instead implements its own minimal character-level scan, never delegating to the host's own path parser, so its behavior — and its test suite — is IDENTICAL regardless of which OS `cargo test` happens to run on. It unconditionally rejects: an empty string; the profile equal to exactly `.` or `..`; any embedded NUL byte; any `/` or `\` character anywhere in the string (BOTH separators, on EVERY host, not just the host's own convention — this alone also rejects a UNC prefix such as `\\server\share` or `//server/share`); any `:` character anywhere (a Windows drive-letter prefix `C:` and an NTFS Alternate Data Stream suffix `name:$DATA` both use `:`, rejected unconditionally rather than trying to distinguish the two shapes, since a profile name has no legitimate use for a colon); a trailing `.` or a trailing space (both are silently stripped by the Windows shell and several Win32 APIs, so a name that LOOKS distinct from an existing one could otherwise collide with it on disk); and the Windows reserved device-name set — cited by reference to **ADR-0021 §9 as the single, authoritative, 30-name list** rather than restated here as a bare count that would drift (Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #3, superseding an earlier "24 names" figure) — matched case-insensitively against the profile's STEM (the substring before the first `.`, if any) after trimming any LEADING spaces from the stem (`" CON"` → `"CON"` → reserved; a leading-space-prefixed NON-reserved name, e.g. `" my-profile"`, has no separate rejection reason and remains accepted). By the time this check runs, a TRAILING dot/space has already been independently rejected above — that remains a distinct hazard, unrelated to the reserved-name list — so only leading-space trimming is needed for the stem comparison itself.

**Preconditions**:
1. `auth_windows_store::file_path(profile)` (or an equivalent path-construction step reachable from `store_pair`/`load_pair`/`remove_if_present`) is invoked with a `Profile` value derived from `Profile::from(String)` — any string, unsanitized (ADR-0011).

**Postconditions**:
1. A profile name containing an embedded `/` or `\` character — BOTH separators are rejected on EVERY host, regardless of the host's own path-separator convention — is REJECTED by `reject_unsafe_profile_component` before any file-system operation (write, read, or delete) is attempted against the resulting path. This alone also rejects a UNC prefix (`\\server\share` or `//server/share`) and any traversal attempt using either separator, identically on Linux, macOS, and Windows CI.
2. A profile name containing a `:` character anywhere is REJECTED — this covers a Windows drive-letter prefix (`C:`) and an NTFS Alternate Data Stream suffix (`name:$DATA`) uniformly; the guard does not attempt to distinguish the two shapes, since a profile name has no legitimate use for a colon.
3. A profile name that is empty, is exactly `.` or `..` (the FULL string, not merely containing them as a substring), or contains an embedded NUL byte is REJECTED.
4. A profile name ending in a trailing `.` or a trailing space is REJECTED — both are silently stripped by the Windows shell and several Win32 APIs, which could otherwise let a name that LOOKS distinct from an existing one collide with it on disk.
5. A profile name that is, or whose STEM (the substring before the first `.`, if any, with any LEADING spaces trimmed before comparison) case-insensitively matches, a Windows reserved device name — the complete 30-name set defined in **ADR-0021 §9** (the 6 classic names, `COM1`-`COM9`, `LPT1`-`LPT9`, and the six Unicode superscript-digit `COM¹`/`COM²`/`COM³`/`LPT¹`/`LPT²`/`LPT³` variants; cited by reference here rather than restated as a bare count, per Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #3) — is REJECTED under the same guard, since these names address a device rather than a regular file on Windows regardless of the intended directory or any trailing extension. A leading space alone, on an otherwise NON-reserved stem, is NOT a rejection trigger — it is absorbed into this stem-normalization step only, unlike a TRAILING dot/space, which remains an independent hazard rejected outright by Postcondition 4.
6. Every rejection above (Postconditions 1-5) surfaces as the typed `ProfilePathEscape` marker error (`src/api/auth_windows_store.rs`, mirroring `DpapiFallbackFailed`'s type-based, never-string-matched discrimination convention), which the CLI layer maps to a distinct, actionable `JrError::UserError` (exit 64) naming the invalid profile name — never a silent no-op and never a successful write to an unintended location.
7. A profile name containing none of the above (the overwhelming common case — alphanumeric, `_`, `-`, and similar ordinary identifier characters) is UNAFFECTED — this BC adds a rejection path, not a new success-path behavior for ordinary profile names.
8. **Cross-platform guard-invocation guarantee (Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #2, ADR-0021 §3/§9):** on EVERY supported OS, `store_pair`, `load_pair`, and `remove_if_present` each invoke the profile-name guard (`reject_unsafe_profile_component`, via `file_path`) as their first action, before any other behavior — including each function's own OS-specific short-circuit (the DPAPI-unavailable honest-fail on `store_pair`, the no-file `Ok(None)` on `load_pair`, and the no-op `Ok(())` on `remove_if_present`, all on a non-Windows build). Concretely, the non-Windows `#[cfg(not(windows))]` arm of each of the three functions ALSO calls `file_path(profile)?` as its literal first statement before its own early return — the resulting `PathBuf` is discarded on non-Windows (never used for I/O there), but `Err(ProfilePathEscape)` propagates identically to the Windows arm. A profile name that fails the guard is therefore rejected IDENTICALLY on every OS, before any of these OS-specific behaviors run — this makes the guard's WIRING (not merely the guard's own correctness, already covered by VP-AUTHDX-016's pure-recognizer proof) regression-catchable on an ordinary Linux/macOS `cargo test` run, closing a gap where a Windows-only regression dropping the guard call from an entry point would previously have passed every default-CI test undetected.

**Invariants**:
1. This guard is a DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH addition specific to the new secret-file artifact this cycle introduces — it does NOT retroactively change `cache_dir(profile)`'s existing (unsanitized) behavior for the disposable, non-secret cache directory; that remains an accepted, separately-tracked residual, unchanged by this BC.
2. Profile names remain operator-controlled local configuration, not remote-attacker-controlled input — this is a hardening measure appropriate to the elevated sensitivity of a SECRET file, not a response to a demonstrated remote-exploitation vector.
3. **Rejection is achieved by CONSTRUCTION, not by post-hoc normalize-and-compare (Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #1).** Every character or shape that could ever be interpreted as a path separator, anchor, drive marker, or escape on ANY target OS is rejected outright by `reject_unsafe_profile_component`'s character-level scan — so any profile-name string that PASSES the guard is, by that fact alone, already guaranteed to be a single, non-empty, non-dot-segment, no-separator, no-colon, no-NUL, no-trailing-dot-or-space, non-reserved-device-name opaque path segment. There is no separate lexical-normalization or containment-comparison step performed after the fact — the guard's exhaustive rejection list IS the containment guarantee.

**Edge Cases**:
- EC-1.4.040-1: Profile name `"../../etc/passwd"` → rejected because it contains `/` (Postcondition 1); the exact string `".."` alone is additionally, and independently, rejected as a dot-segment (Postcondition 3) — both vectors are covered, regardless of which of the three DPAPI-store functions is invoked.
- EC-1.4.040-2: Profile name containing a raw path separator without a `..` component (e.g. `"sub/dir"`) → still rejected via Postcondition 1's unconditional separator check, since `reject_unsafe_profile_component` rejects `/`/`\` outright rather than only when paired with a traversal component.
- EC-1.4.040-3: An ordinary profile name (e.g. `"default"`, `"sandbox-prod"`, `"team_a"`) → succeeds exactly as before (Postcondition 7); this BC introduces zero behavior change for any profile name already in normal use across this codebase's existing test fixtures.
- EC-1.4.040-4: A profile name that is empty, or exactly `"."`/`".."` in full (not merely containing those as a substring) → rejected under Postcondition 3 (an empty or bare-dot component is not a valid directory-segment identity for a per-profile secret directory).
- EC-1.4.040-5: A profile name containing a Windows drive-letter prefix (`"C:"`, `"C:\\evil"`) or an NTFS Alternate-Data-Stream colon (`"secret:$DATA"`) → rejected per Postcondition 2 (the unconditional `:` check); the DPAPI secret file is Windows-only (Finding #5's `#[cfg(windows)]`-gated engagement), so this vector is reachable on the file's only real platform and must not be omitted from the guard's enumeration.
- EC-1.4.040-6: A profile name that is a UNC path prefix (`"\\\\server\\share"`) → rejected per Postcondition 1 — a UNC prefix is itself composed of `\` (or `/`) characters, so the same unconditional separator check that catches embedded traversal also catches this vector with no special-casing required.
- EC-1.4.040-7: A profile name equal to (or whose stem before the first `.`, leading spaces trimmed, matches) a Windows reserved device name from **ADR-0021 §9's 30-name set** — including the classic `"CON"`/`"PRN"`/`"AUX"`/`"NUL"`/`"CONIN$"`/`"CONOUT$"`/`"COM1"`-`"COM9"`/`"LPT1"`-`"LPT9"` AND the Unicode superscript-digit variants `"COM¹"`/`"COM²"`/`"COM³"`/`"LPT¹"`/`"LPT²"`/`"LPT³"` (case-insensitive; with or without a trailing extension, e.g. `"con.txt"`) → rejected per Postcondition 5, since Windows resolves these as device handles rather than regular files regardless of directory intent.
- EC-1.4.040-8: A profile name containing an embedded NUL byte (`"secret\0name"`) → rejected per Postcondition 3, before the string ever reaches a filesystem call that could otherwise truncate it at the NUL and silently address a different (shorter) path than the one that was validated.
- EC-1.4.040-9: A profile name ending in a trailing `.` or a trailing space (e.g. `"profile."`, `"profile "`) → rejected per Postcondition 4; without this check, such a name would be indistinguishable on disk from its non-trailing counterpart (`"profile"`) once the Windows shell or a Win32 API silently strips the trailing character, letting two apparently-distinct profile names collide on the same secret file.
- EC-1.4.040-10 (Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #3): A profile name with a LEADING space in front of a reserved-device-name stem (e.g. `" CON"`, `" LPT1"`) → rejected per Postcondition 5, because the stem comparison trims leading spaces before matching (`" CON"` → `"CON"` → reserved); a leading-space-prefixed NON-reserved name (e.g. `" my-profile"`) has no separate rejection reason under this guard and is accepted — the leading-space handling exists solely to close this reserved-device-name evasion, not as an independent hazard the way a TRAILING dot/space is (Postcondition 4).

**Verification Properties**:
- **VP-AUTHDX-016 — Profile-name-as-filename path-traversal guard on the secret file (DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH, cross-platform / PURE, NEW 2026-09-03, cycle-004 F2 VP-delta pass; class DOWNGRADED from "SECURITY INVARIANT / HIGH PRIORITY / CWE-22" to DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH per Pass-20 gate-audit adversarial review (2026-09-04, architect guidance items 1-2, Finding #1 HIGH): the PRIMARY, live gate is `validate_profile_name` (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005, `src/config.rs`) — a strict superset of this guard's 30 vectors, restricting every profile name reaching a credential-storage call site to ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` ≤64 chars with reserved-Windows-names excluded, enforced at config-load (both loaders) and at the CLI-flag/resolved-active-profile-name boundary BEFORE any subcommand dispatches — so it, not this recognizer, already makes every one of these vectors unreachable via any normal CLI/config path; this VP's guard `reject_unsafe_profile_component` BACKSTOPS that gate against (a) a FUTURE relaxation of `validate_profile_name`'s charset/reserved-name list or (b) a validation-call-site regression bypassing BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005 — NOT a live CWE-22 vulnerability. The asserted property below — the 30-vector + separator/colon/NUL/trailing-dot-or-space rejection, as a typed `ProfilePathEscape` before any FS op, at all three store entry points on both cfg arms, PLUS the DESIGN-CONFORMANCE and guard-WIRING sub-oracles — is UNCHANGED and still fully verified; ONLY the priority/threat-model framing changes; Windows-vector enumeration corrected Pass-1 adversarial review Finding #2; guard mechanism re-specified from "lexical-normalize-then-containment" to the host-independent character-level recognizer `reject_unsafe_profile_component` per Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #1, ADR-0021 §9; reserved-device-name set finalized to the authoritative 30-name list of ADR-0021 §9 per Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #3, and a DISTINCT guard-WIRING oracle exercising all three entry points on the default runner added per Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #2).** Unit under test: `reject_unsafe_profile_component(profile: &str) -> Result<(), ProfilePathEscape>` (`src/api/auth_windows_store.rs`) — the guard invoked as `file_path`'s FIRST statement and thereby reached, before any FS op, from ALL THREE DPAPI-store entry points (`store_pair`/`load_pair`/`remove_if_present` — Postcondition 8). Property: for ANY profile-name string derived from the unsanitized `Profile::from(String)` (ADR-0011): (a) **acceptance ⇒ opaque single segment:** a name that PASSES the recognizer is, by that fact alone, a single non-empty, non-dot-segment, no-separator, no-colon, no-NUL, no-trailing-dot-or-space, non-reserved-device-name opaque path segment (Invariant 3) — the exhaustive character-level rejection list IS the containment guarantee, with NO post-hoc lexical-normalize-and-compare step (Postcondition 1); (b) **rejection-before-I/O, every enumerated vector — asserted on ANY host:** each of — an embedded `/` OR `\` separator (EC-1.4.040-1/2, BOTH separators on EVERY host, which also catches a UNC prefix via either separator, EC-1.4.040-6); a `:` character anywhere (a drive-letter prefix such as `C:` AND an NTFS Alternate-Data-Stream suffix such as `name:$DATA`, EC-1.4.040-5); an empty string, an exact `.` or `..`, or an embedded NUL byte (EC-1.4.040-4/8); a trailing `.` or trailing space (EC-1.4.040-9); and a Windows reserved device name matched case-insensitively against the STEM before the first `.` (with or without extension, e.g. `con.txt`), after trimming any LEADING spaces from the stem (`" CON"` → `"CON"`, EC-1.4.040-10) — the authoritative **30-name** set defined in **ADR-0021 §9** (cited by reference, not restated as a bare count that would drift, per Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #3, superseding the earlier "24 names" figure): the 6 classic names `CON`/`PRN`/`AUX`/`NUL`/`CONIN$`/`CONOUT$`, `COM1`-`COM9`, `LPT1`-`LPT9`, and the 6 Unicode superscript-digit variants `COM¹`/`COM²`/`COM³`/`LPT¹`/`LPT²`/`LPT³` (U+00B9/U+00B2/U+00B3) — the enumerated inputs including the console pseudo-handles `CONIN$`/`CONOUT$`, a trailing-dot/space form such as `CON.`/`CON ` (the stem match runs AFTER the trailing-dot/space rejection, EC-1.4.040-7), a superscript-digit variant such as `COM¹`, and a leading-space-prefixed reserved stem such as `" CON"` (EC-1.4.040-10) — is REJECTED as the typed `ProfilePathEscape` marker error (never string-matched, mirroring `DpapiFallbackFailed`), which the CLI maps to a distinct `JrError::UserError` (exit 64) naming the invalid profile name, BEFORE any filesystem write/read/delete is attempted against the resulting path (Postconditions 1-6); (c) **no regression for ordinary names:** an alphanumeric/`_`/`-` profile name (`"default"`, `"sandbox-prod"`, `"team_a"`), AND a leading-space-prefixed NON-reserved name (`" my-profile"`, whose leading space is absorbed ONLY into the reserved-stem normalization and is not itself a rejection trigger, EC-1.4.040-10), is accepted unchanged — zero behavior change for names already in existing fixtures (Postcondition 7, EC-1.4.040-3); (d) **cross-platform guard-WIRING oracle — a SEPARATE property from the recognizer's own correctness (a)-(c) (Pass-4 adversarial review Finding #2, Postcondition 8):** the guard is not merely CORRECT in isolation, it is actually INVOKED at all three store entry points on every OS. On a default (Linux/macOS) CI runner — with NO `#[cfg(windows)]` gate — calling `store_pair`, `load_pair`, and `remove_if_present` DIRECTLY, each with a profile name that fails `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (e.g. one containing `/`, `:`, or a reserved device name), MUST return `Err` downcastable to `ProfilePathEscape`, emitted BEFORE any filesystem operation AND before each function's own OS-specific short-circuit (`store_pair`'s `DpapiFallbackFailed` honest-fail, `load_pair`'s no-file `Ok(None)`, `remove_if_present`'s no-op `Ok(())`), because ADR-0021 §3/§9 mandate the non-Windows `#[cfg(not(windows))]` arm call `file_path(profile)?` as its literal first statement before its own early return. This catches a Windows-only regression that drops the guard call from ANY one of the three entry points on ordinary `cargo test` — where, before this wiring, the same bad name would have returned a "successful" `Ok(None)`/`Ok(())` or the unrelated `DpapiFallbackFailed` marker, silently swallowing it. This oracle MUST be a SEPARATE test (or sibling test) from the pure-recognizer unit cases (a)-(c), so the recognizer's correctness and the call-site WIRING fail INDEPENDENTLY and legibly if either regresses (it verifies the WIRING, not the recognizer). **Host-independence is an EXPLICIT, asserted oracle property, not an incidental one (Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #1, ADR-0021 §9):** every Windows-shaped vector above (drive-letter colon, ADS colon, UNC prefix via either separator, and each of the 30 reserved device names — including the Unicode superscript variants `COM¹`/`LPT¹` and the leading-space form `" CON"`) MUST be asserted REJECTED by the SAME test running on the default (Linux/macOS) CI runner, with NO `#[cfg(windows)]` gate on the assertion — so a green run on the Linux CI runner ITSELF proves the recognizer, not the host OS, does the rejecting; the test carries a doc-comment/name recording this ("asserted without any `#[cfg(windows)]` gate — the recognizer, not the host OS, does the work"). **DESIGN-CONFORMANCE assertion (Pass-2 Finding #1) — a future `std::path` substitution is caught by THIS VP, not silently passed:** the harness MUST include an assertion that FAILS if the guard is re-implemented via `std::path::Path`/`Component`; concretely, the drive-letter, UNC, and ADS-colon vectors are asserted REJECTED on the non-Windows CI host, where a `std::path`-based implementation would wrongly ACCEPT them (each parses to a single opaque `Component::Normal` lexically contained under `secrets/`, the exact opposite of intent). **Mandated guard shape — a host-independent, character-level recognizer applied before any FS op at all three entry points; explicitly NOT `std::path` and NOT a post-hoc normalize-and-compare (Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #1, ADR-0021 §9):** `reject_unsafe_profile_component` performs its OWN minimal character-level scan and NEVER delegates to `std::path::Path`/`Component`, whose parsing is scoped to the host OS and would, on a Linux/macOS CI runner, treat a drive-letter prefix, a UNC prefix, or an NTFS ADS-colon name as a single opaque `Component::Normal` lexically contained under `secrets/` and wrongly ACCEPT it. The recognizer unconditionally rejects, by construction: an empty string; the profile equal to exactly `.` or `..`; any embedded NUL byte; any `/` or `\` character anywhere (BOTH separators, on EVERY host — this alone also catches a UNC prefix via either separator); any `:` character anywhere (drive-letter prefix and NTFS ADS colon alike); a trailing `.` or trailing space; and — matched case-insensitively against the STEM before the first `.`, after LEADING-space trimming (`" CON"` → `"CON"`), and AFTER the trailing-dot/space rejection — the authoritative 30-name Windows reserved device set defined in **ADR-0021 §9** (cited by reference, not restated as a drifting count; the 6 classic names, `COM1`-`COM9`, `LPT1`-`LPT9`, and the 6 Unicode superscript-digit `COM¹`/`COM²`/`COM³`/`LPT¹`/`LPT²`/`LPT³` variants). Any profile name that PASSES is therefore already a single opaque path segment (Invariant 3) — the exhaustive rejection list IS the containment guarantee, so there is deliberately NO separate lexical-normalization or containment-comparison step layered after the fact. All checks run BEFORE any filesystem write/read/delete, at ALL THREE entry points (`store_pair`/`load_pair`/`remove_if_present`, Postcondition 8) through the single `file_path` call site — a hostile profile name is rejected at path-construction time regardless of which of the three operations invoked it. This is a DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH guard specific to the new SECRET-file artifact; it does NOT retroactively change `cache_dir(profile)`'s existing unsanitized behavior for the disposable cache directory (Invariant 1). **Verification method**: property test (`proptest`, arbitrary profile-name strings including adversarial traversal payloads covering both Unix- and Windows-shaped vectors) PLUS enumerated unit cases for the 30 reserved device names cited from ADR-0021 §9 (incl. `CONIN$`/`CONOUT$`, the Unicode superscript-digit variants `COM¹`/`LPT¹`, trailing-dot/space forms `CON.`/`CON `, and the leading-space form `" CON"`) and the colon, NUL, UNC, and both-separator vectors, mirroring the existing `prop_sanitize_attachment_filename_no_path_traversal` CWE-22 discipline (CLAUDE.md `sanitize_attachment_filename` convention); PLUS a SEPARATE guard-WIRING test (property (d)) that calls `store_pair`/`load_pair`/`remove_if_present` DIRECTLY with a guard-failing profile name and asserts each returns `Err` downcastable to `ProfilePathEscape` before any FS op or OS-specific short-circuit — fully cross-platform, runs in default CI (pure character-level check, no DPAPI/Windows dependency), with the host-independence property, the `std::path`-substitution DESIGN-CONFORMANCE assertion, AND the guard-WIRING oracle ALL asserted on the Linux/macOS runner (no `#[cfg(windows)]` gate). **F6 target**: `src/api/auth_windows_store.rs::reject_unsafe_profile_component` (the recognizer under test), `::file_path` (its sole call site), and `::store_pair`/`::load_pair`/`::remove_if_present` (the three entry points whose non-Windows cfg arms must each invoke `file_path` first — the guard-WIRING oracle's units under test).

**Related BCs**: BC-1.4.037 (the file this BC's guard protects), BC-1.4.035 (the `#[cfg(windows)]`-gated engagement, Finding #5, that makes this file — and therefore this guard's Windows-syntax vectors — reachable only on Windows), `src/cache.rs::cache_dir` (the pre-existing, unmodified, unsanitized profile-name-join precedent this BC's guard mirrors in shape but elevates for the SECRET-file case; see Finding #6 — this is NOT BC-6.2.016, which documents `cache_root()`'s Windows path resolution, not `cache_dir(profile)`'s join), BC-6.1.004 (`validate_profile_name` charset/length/reserved-name rejection — the PRIMARY, live gate this guard sits behind as defense-in-depth, Pass-20 gate-audit correction, 2026-09-04), BC-6.1.005 (validation runs at config-load, both strict and lenient loaders, and at the CLI-flag/resolved-active-profile-name boundary — Pass-20 gate-audit correction).

**Architecture Anchors**: ADR-0021 §9 (`reject_unsafe_profile_component`, the concrete host-independent recognizer this BC's postconditions specify — Pass-2 adversarial review Finding #1); architecture-delta.md §9 item 1 (residual design question raised by the architect for this exact concern).

**Story Anchor**: TBD (F3)

**VP Anchors**: VP-AUTHDX-016

**Trace**: F2 spec evolution, cycle-004 `windows-correctness` (2026-09-03), issue #759, DEC-334; architecture-delta.md §9 item 1; product-owner F2 decision: authored as a DEDICATED BC (not an EC on BC-1.4.037) because the guard is shared across all three DPAPI-store entry points (store/load/remove), not solely the write path, and warrants its own VP hook independent of the atomic-write mechanism BC-1.4.037 governs; cross-reference `sanitize_attachment_filename` (CWE-22 discipline precedent, CLAUDE.md), ADR-0011 (`Profile::from(String)` "Infallible by design"). Pass-2 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-03): Finding #1 (HIGH) — guard requirement reworded from ambiguous "Windows-syntax-aware" to the concrete, host-independent recognizer `reject_unsafe_profile_component` (ADR-0021 §9), explicitly NOT `std::path`-based, restated as an exhaustive character-level rejection list (Postconditions 1-8) rather than a normalize-and-containment description; new EC-1.4.040-8/9 (NUL byte, trailing dot/space). Finding #4 (MED) — reserved device-name set finalized to include `CONIN$`/`CONOUT$` alongside the classic DOS names, stem-matched after trailing-dot/space rejection. Pass-4 adversarial review fix round (2026-09-03): Finding #2 (MED) — Postcondition 8 reworded to state the guard-invocation guarantee as an explicit CROSS-PLATFORM contract (both cfg arms of `store_pair`/`load_pair`/`remove_if_present` invoke the guard as their literal first statement, per ADR-0021 §3/§9), making the wiring regression-catchable on default Linux/macOS CI rather than provable only by inspection. Finding #3 (LOW) — reserved device-name set finalized to 30 names (ADR-0021 §9, cited by reference rather than restated as a bare count) adding the six Unicode superscript-digit `COM`/`LPT` variants, plus leading-space trimming of the stem before matching; new EC-1.4.040-10. Pass-20 / gate-audit adversarial review fix round (2026-09-04, Finding #1, HIGH — cross-corpus consistency / mischaracterized-threat-model, NOT a design or behavior defect): Confidence/Source/Origin/Description reclassified — the guard was framed as "a new hardening requirement escalated from an inherited, previously-undocumented risk," implying "there is NO profile-name validation today"; this is FALSE: `validate_profile_name` (BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005, `src/config.rs`) already restricts every profile name reaching any credential-storage call site to ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` ≤64 chars with reserved-Windows-names excluded, enforced at config-load (both loaders) and at the CLI-flag/resolved-active-profile-name boundary, BEFORE any subcommand dispatches — a strict superset of the 30 vectors this guard rejects, making every one of them already unreachable via any normal CLI/config path. Reworded to state the guard is DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH against (a) a future relaxation of `validate_profile_name`'s charset/reserved-name list and (b) a validation-call-site regression bypassing BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005 — not a live CWE-22 closure. New "Related BCs" cross-reference to BC-6.1.004 (the charset/length/reserved-name rule) and BC-6.1.005 (the three enforcement boundaries) as the PRIMARY, live gate this guard sits behind. **No postcondition, invariant, edge case, or guard behavior changed** — `reject_unsafe_profile_component`, `file_path`, and their 30-vector rejection set are UNMODIFIED; this is a rationale/priority-framing and cross-reference correction only. No `#### BC-` heading added or removed; total_bcs (80) and definitional_count (69) UNCHANGED. VP-AUTHDX-016's "SECURITY INVARIANT, HIGH PRIORITY, CWE-22" label downgrade to DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH, and its BC-6.1.004/BC-6.1.005 cross-reference, per architect's Pass-20 guidance item 1-2 ("For the formal-verifier"), WERE APPLIED IN THIS FILE — formal-verifier downgraded VP-AUTHDX-016 inline (Pass-20 gate-audit, 2026-09-04; see the VP's "class DOWNGRADED … to DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH" text above), consistent with `vp-delta.md` line 61 and BC-INDEX; the VP-016 assertion itself, its 30-vector coverage, and its test count are unaffected either way. ### 1.5 OAuth State Machine

#### BC-1.5.031: Embedded OAuth callback URL is exactly `http://127.0.0.1:53682/callback`

**Confidence**: HIGH
**Source**: `src/api/auth.rs:~374`; CLAUDE.md; ADR-0006
**Subject**: Auth & Identity
**Behavior**: `EMBEDDED_CALLBACK_PORT: u16 = 53682`. IPv4 literal `127.0.0.1` (NOT `localhost` — avoids macOS/Chrome `localhost`→`::1` resolver pitfall). Atlassian validates `redirect_uri` by EXACT string match. Changing this is a breaking release.
**Trace**: Pass 3 BC-031 (R1); BC-1140/1141 (R4)

**Confidence**: HIGH
**Source**: `src/api/auth.rs:~427`
**Subject**: Auth & Identity

Set --client-id/--client-secret (or set JR_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID/JR_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET) to fall back to a dynamic port."` Contains 5 substrings: `port 53682 is in use`, `the jr OAuth callback needs this port`, `--client-id/--client-secret`, `JR_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID/JR_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET`, `dynamic port`.
**Trace**: Pass 3 BC-032 (R1); BC-1161 (R4)

### 1.6 Auth Error Handling & 401 Dispatch

#### BC-1.6.042: 401 + `scope does not match` body → InsufficientScope with 5 required substrings

**Confidence**: HIGH
**Source**: `tests/api_client.rs:~99`
**Subject**: Auth & Identity
**Behavior**: 401 body containing `scope does not match` (case-insensitive) → `JrError::InsufficientScope`. Display MUST contain: `Insufficient token scope`, raw gateway message, **the resolved required scope name** (`write:jira-work` when `required_scope` is `None`, otherwise the call-site-supplied scope name such as `write:servicedesk-request`), `OAuth 2.0`, `github.com/Zious11/jira-cli/issues/185`. Exit code 2.

**Empty-Some policy:** Construction sites MUST pass either `None` or `Some(s)` where `s` is a non-empty ASCII scope name. To enforce this defensively, the Display impl treats `Some("")` identically to `None` — i.e., falls back to `write:jira-work`. The thiserror template MUST use `.filter(|s| !s.is_empty())` between `as_deref()` and `unwrap_or` to enforce this. A unit test MUST pin `Some("")` → fallback behavior.

**Trace**: Pass 3 BC-015; BC-1085 (R4); Top-30 BC rank #1
**Change**: [MODIFIED 2026-05-19 issue #382] Parameterized substring #3 to support runtime-resolved scope name via `JrError::InsufficientScope { required_scope: Option<String> }` field. Backward-compatible: `None` branch preserves historical literal `write:jira-work`. [MODIFIED 2026-05-19 issue #382 pass-02] Added Empty-Some policy: Some("") treated as None per defensive fallback.

#### BC-1.6.046: `auth list` table snapshot: 5 columns (NAME, URL, ENV, AUTH, STATUS), active profile with `* ` prefix

**STATUS: AMENDED (2026-09-01, cycle-003 `auth-profile-dx`, DEC-324) — DELIBERATE, ACKNOWLEDGED BREAKING CHANGE** to the previously-pinned 4-column insta-snapshot. The `env` tag (DEC-314) is added as a table column rather than left JSON-only, resolving F1 Open Question 7 at the architecture level (ADR-0020 §Decision 4): the tag's entire purpose — distinguishing prod/sandbox/uat profiles at a glance — is defeated if it is invisible in the one place a human scans all profiles side-by-side.

**Confidence**: HIGH
**Source**: `src/cli/auth/tests/snapshots/jr__cli__auth__tests__list_table_snapshot.snap` (snapshot regeneration is an F4 implementation-story deliverable, not performed by this F2 spec pass); ADR-0020 §Decision 4
**Subject**: Auth & Identity
**Behavior**: Columns: `NAME, URL, ENV, AUTH, STATUS` — `ENV` inserted between `URL` and `AUTH` (grouping the two profile-identity/environment-facing columns before the two mechanism/health columns). Active profile prefixed `* ` (asterisk-space). Inactive: `  ` (2 spaces). `ENV` cell shows the profile's `env` value verbatim when set (subject to the display-sanitization transform in EC-1.6.046-2), or an empty/placeholder cell when unset — see EC-1.6.046-1 for the exact rendering contract. 3-profile fixture (to be extended in F4 with at least one `env`-tagged profile): default* (api_token), sandbox (oauth), staging (api_token). All STATUS cells `configured`.

**Ownership (H-2, adversary pass-2 fix):** This BC (together with BC-1.6.047's JSON-channel counterpart) is the OWNER of the `env` display-sanitization requirement bc-6's BC-6.1.015 cross-references — bc-6 owns the `ProfileConfig.env: Option<String>` STORAGE contract (raw, lossless, no sanitization at that layer); this BC owns the TERMINAL/human-table DISPLAY-layer sanitization behavior specifically (EC-1.6.046-2). BC-1.6.047 owns the companion JSON-channel behavior (verbatim/lossless, per #398's machine-channel convention) and the human-text (`auth status`) channel's sanitization.

**Previous version (superseded by DEC-324, retained for audit trail):**
> **Behavior**: Columns: `NAME, URL, AUTH, STATUS`. Active profile prefixed `* ` (asterisk-space). Inactive: `  ` (2 spaces). 3-profile fixture: default* (api_token), sandbox (oauth), staging (api_token). All STATUS cells `configured`.

**Edge Cases**:
- EC-1.6.046-1 (O-4, F2-gate fix): the human-table `ENV` cell's blank-vs-placeholder rendering MUST match the JSON contract's `""`-vs-`null` distinction from BC-1.6.047 — these are two OBSERVABLY DIFFERENT states, not one collapsed display. Concretely: `env: Some("")` (a profile whose `env` field is explicitly set to an empty string — e.g. via a hand-edited `config.toml` `env = ""`) renders as a genuinely BLANK cell (zero visible characters between the column's cell boundaries, same as any other column's blank content would render); `env: None` (the field absent/unset entirely — the common case for a profile predating this cycle, or one that never had `env` set) renders with a visible PLACEHOLDER character, `-` (a single hyphen), so a human scanning the table can distinguish "this profile was explicitly tagged with an empty string" from "this profile was never tagged at all." This replaces the original draft's punt ("exact placeholder text… is an F4 implementation-story detail, not fixed by this BC") — the distinction is now fixed at the spec layer because it is the human-table's one required parity point with BC-1.6.047's JSON `""`-vs-`null` contract, not an arbitrary cosmetic choice left to the implementer.
- EC-1.6.046-2 (NEW, H-2, adversary pass-2 fix — display-layer sanitization, resolves the contradiction with bc-6's BC-6.1.015 cross-reference): before a non-empty `env` value is written into the `ENV` cell, it is CONTROL-CHARACTER/ANSI-ESCAPE-STRIPPED and LENGTH-CAPPED — mirroring the class of display-safety transform this corpus already applies elsewhere (`display_sanitize_filename`'s CWE-116 transform, S-576-1), applied here to a different field for the identical reason (an untrusted/hand-edited config value must never corrupt the rendered table or inject terminal control sequences). Concretely: (a) ASCII control characters (`0x00`–`0x1F`, `0x7F`) and ANSI CSI/OSC escape sequences are stripped from the value before insertion into the `comfy-table` cell; (b) the sanitized value is capped to a fixed maximum display length, with a truncation marker appended when capped. This is a DISPLAY-layer transform ONLY — the value bc-6's BC-6.1.015 stores in `config.toml` and returns from `ProfileConfig.env` is NEVER modified, truncated, or rejected by this transform; only the rendered TABLE CELL's bytes are sanitized, at the point this BC's table-formatting code path consumes the stored value. This closes the H-2 contradiction: bc-6's BC-6.1.015 cites this BC (not itself) as the display-sanitization owner, and prior to this EC that citation resolved to a BC that stated the opposite ("displayed verbatim, no rejection, no truncation" — see BC-1.6.047 EC-1.6.047-1, now scoped to the JSON channel only).

**Trace**: Pass 3 BC-1115 (R4); F2 spec evolution, cycle-003 `auth-profile-dx` (2026-09-01, DEC-314/DEC-324) — 5th column added, ADR-0020 §Decision 4 resolves F1 Open Question 7; F2-gate fix (2026-09-01, O-4) — EC-1.6.046-1 added, fixing the `Some("")`-vs-`None` table-rendering distinction (blank vs. `-` placeholder) to match BC-1.6.047's JSON `""`-vs-`null` contract, replacing the prior "F4 implementation-story detail" punt; adversary pass-2 fix (2026-09-01, H-2) — Ownership clause and EC-1.6.046-2 added, resolving the contradiction with bc-6's BC-6.1.015 cross-reference by making this BC the actual owner of the terminal/human-table display-sanitization contract (control-character/ANSI-escape strip + length cap), scoped to the display layer only — storage stays verbatim per bc-6's BC-6.1.015; cross-reference BC-1.6.047 (AMENDED, H-2 — the JSON-shape sibling contract for `env` display, split into JSON-verbatim vs. human-text-sanitized channels, and the `""`-vs-`null` distinction EC-1.6.046-1 mirrors); the actual insta snapshot file is regenerated as part of the F4 implementing story, not by this spec pass. **Cross-ref confirmed (integrate pass, 2026-09-01):** bc-6's BC-6.1.015 (`ProfileConfig.env: Option<String>`, `bc-6-config-cache.md` §6.1) cites this BC and BC-1.6.047 as its display-layer consumers (see its "Cross-reference (bc-1, not authored here)" clause) — this BC and BC-1.6.047 are confirmed (adversary pass-2, H-2) to actually own that requirement as of EC-1.6.046-2 and BC-1.6.047's channel split, resolving the previous contradiction.
